- **Executable evidence at this exact working tree, each run and watched:** `npx tsc -b --force` exited `0`. `node scripts/foundation-static-guards.mjs` → `Foundation static guards: PASS`, with five pins deleted because the code they described no longer exists and nineteen added or re-pointed. `node scripts/verify-foundation-mutations.mjs` → `Foundation mutation drill: PASS (74 controlled mutations turned the gate red)`, up from 59 at `7dacc77`, every new label reported by name. `scripts/maple-season-browser-port-preflight.regression.ps1` → `MAPLE_SEASON_BROWSER_PORT_PREFLIGHT_REGRESSION_PASS`, exit `0`, in six seconds, now including the thirty-case Win32 equivalence table and the stall drill. The twenty-two-case predicate probe returned `as-required` on all twenty-two, and the three-case stall control returned `COMPLETED -> 3`, `STALLED`, `COMPLETED -> 2` in that order. The full gate, `powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File scripts/verify-foundation.ps1`, was re-run after the last edit to any file and printed `Farm Rx foundation gate: PASS` with `62 passed (46.2s)` and 14 intentional skips.
- **Honestly unfixed, and named rather than counted as done:** nine of Sol's sixteen findings remain open and are the next tranche — unnamed cleanup errors (F7); the one-second start-time tolerance possibly rejecting a legitimate listener (F8, SUSPECT); the purity pin's case-sensitivity and its missing vocabulary, `$ExecutionContext`, `Get-Location`, `Resolve-Path`, `$env:`, and the .NET filesystem APIs (F10); a decoy accounting region defeating the slice (F11); the drill's own completion and count logic being unpinned (F12); `Stop-Process -Id` being free to reappear in cleanup, and a workflow step being free to carry `if: false` (F13); cleanup being invoked without first asserting exactly one listener on the port (F14); and `WaitForExit`'s boolean going unchecked (F15). F16 is rejected on measurement: the foreign-listener case uses a generated `farmrx-not-this-tree-<guid>` path that is never created, so Sol's "a real tree" is wrong. Finding 4's three remaining classes — a foreign process merely naming our root, a sibling worktree under our root, and a junction or symlink spelling of the same tree — still answer TRUE, because the command-line string genuinely cannot distinguish them; the Job Object proposal above is the answer, and it is Mason's decision. Nine `scripts/*.regression.ps1` files remain orphaned with no blanket caller guard, `main`'s coverage of the predicate is still text plus the runtime drill's five non-Windows cases because the runner is `ubuntu-latest` and `Get-NetTCPConnection` is Windows-only, the predicate's process test is still the image name, and the preflight message's mixed owned-and-foreign branch is still untested.
- **Boundary:** all work was local. Nothing was pushed, opened as a pull request, merged, or deployed. No migration was applied to any database, and no database was touched beyond the disposable local project `farmrx-farmer-simplicity-2027-local` used by the gate's existing probes. No live data, Auth, permission, secret, customer account, or customer communication was touched, and `C:\CRX_Manager` was not modified. PID `22580` was left running throughout and verified still running after the gate; its command line is the one the fourth hole would have authorized killing, and the twenty-two-case probe includes it as a case that must answer FALSE. This commit awaits its own fresh-context read-only Sol review and claims no acceptance. Farm Rx remains **RELEASE CANDIDATE READY**, not **COMPLETE**; both physical installed-PWA phone journeys remain open owner-operated gates documented in `docs/customer-zero-readiness-runbook.md`, and weather-to-spray remains manual payload-free transcription without provenance, integration, or automatic prefill.

## SR-067 — Sol REQUEST-CHANGES on `2321904` repaired: the guards themselves measured blind to a predicate that authorizes every kill, a behavioural regression added and wired to two independent callers, and seven of SR-066's claims corrected

- **Date/time:** 2026-08-05 (`America/Chicago`).
- **Authority:** local repair work only. Mason's authorization covers local commits on worktree branch `claude/gauntlet-testing-sweep-013d65`. The earlier repair-plan approval question remains unanswered, the structural decisions raised in SR-062 through SR-066 remain unanswered, and no outward action was taken or is claimed as approved. This entry corrects SR-066 by appending; SR-060 through SR-066 are left exactly as written.
- **Origin:** a fresh-context read-only Sol review of the exact commit `2321904` returned REQUEST-CHANGES with twenty-two findings, five of them BLOCKING, including a truthfulness audit of SR-066. Every finding acted on below was reproduced on this workstation before any edit was made.
- **The finding that mattered most, and it was measured before it was believed.** Sol's F15/F16 said the repository's guards cannot tell a working kill-authorizing predicate from a broken one. That was tested directly: `return $true` was inserted at the top of `Test-MapleSeasonBrowserPortOwned`, which authorizes force-killing **every** listener on the governed port, a foreign process included. `node scripts/foundation-static-guards.mjs` printed `PASS`. `node scripts/verify-foundation-mutations.mjs` printed `PASS (85 controlled mutations turned the gate red)` — all eighty-five detected. Not one pinned substring had moved; only the behaviour was gone. The same lesson that produced four consecutive false-TRUEs in the predicate applies to the guards over it: pinning a string is a reimplementation of "the code still works," and it fails the same way. The only gate that caught the gutted predicate was the Windows-only socket regression, and CI runs `ubuntu-latest`, so `main` had **no** behavioural coverage of this function at all.
- **The fix is a suite that calls the predicate, and proves its own teeth every run.** `scripts/maple-season-browser-ownership.regression.ps1` is new. It slices the tokenizer, the containment test and the predicate out of `maple-season-browser.ps1` (refusing an ambiguous slice, and refusing a slice missing any of the three), then runs a **29-case** tokenizer equivalence table and a **25-case** portable refusal table against them. It then guts the predicate **in memory** — the same `return $true` — and requires the refusal table to reject the gutted copy on an **exact count** rather than "at least one," so the whole table is proven reachable and not merely entered; the tokenizer table gets the same treatment against a tokenizer that returns the command line as one argument. Measured: gutted, the suite reports `FAIL 25 case(s)` on the non-Windows path and `FAIL 35 case(s)` on Windows; intact, `PASS` on Windows PowerShell 5.1, on PowerShell 7.6.3, and with `$onWindows` forced false to simulate the ubuntu runner.
- **Its expectations are measured from Windows and then hard-coded, which is what lets CI run it.** The tokenizer table's expected argument vectors were emitted from real `CommandLineToArgvW` calls and written into the file as literals, so the same 29 cases execute on `ubuntu-latest` where that API does not exist — while the Windows-only socket regression still re-derives them live, so a literal that ever drifts from Windows fails there. Containment is different in kind: `[System.IO.Path]::GetFullPath` is platform-dependent, and on Linux a backslash is an ordinary character, so no Windows-shaped path can resolve inside a Windows-shaped root. The **refusals** — the dangerous direction — therefore run everywhere, and the 24 must-be-TRUE and resolver-dependent cases are gated to Windows with an explicit skip line rather than silently passing.
- **Wired to two independent callers, for the reason the three node gates are listed separately in the workflow.** The suite is a `shell: pwsh` step in `.github/workflows/foundation.yml` **and** a lane in `scripts/verify-foundation.ps1`; both relay the child's output and require its `MAPLE_SEASON_BROWSER_OWNERSHIP_REGRESSION_PASS` marker, because a non-zero exit alone accepts a regression edited to return before proving anything. The orchestrator lane was proven by slicing the real lines out of the real file and executing them against three controls: the true regression relayed `PASS`; a stand-in exiting `0` with no marker raised `Season browser ownership regression did not print its completion marker.`; a stand-in exiting `1` raised `Season browser ownership regression failed.` The workflow step was proven by parsing the YAML (10 steps, this one `shell: pwsh` and carrying no `if:` condition), extracting its `run` body, and executing it from the repository root as CI does: exit `0` against the real regression, exit `1` against both failure modes. Static pins now require both callers, both marker assertions, and the anti-vacuity self-test itself; six new mutations drill them, including one that forges the orchestrator's marker instead of running the child and one that degrades the exact-count assertion to "at least one."
- **Four defects from Sol's earlier findings closed in the predicate and its drill, one of which reverses a claim SR-066 made.** F1: Windows has two argument grammars that disagree on exactly one construct, so a command line containing a doubled quote is now **refused outright** rather than parsed under a guess — measured, shell32 splits `--label "C:\Other"" C:\FarmRx\safe"` so half a label reads as a path in our tree, while node's own C-runtime parse keeps it one argument naming nothing of ours. F2: the backslash branch of the tokenizer no longer decides what a quote means; an even run leaves the quote to the single quote branch, so there is now exactly one place in the function that makes that decision. F4 is the reversal: SR-066 credited the stall guard with "degrading a residual no-progress parse to a short argument list, which can only cost an owned listener its match." **That was wrong, and the truncation was a false-TRUE of its own.** Measured on the drifted separator, `node.exe C:\FarmRx<NBSP>Backup\server.js` truncates to `node.exe`, `C:\FarmRx`, `` — and the bare exact root **is** a containment match, so the truncation authorized killing the sibling's listener. The guard now **throws** and refuses to answer ownership from a partial parse. F5: the stall drill's `Assert-True ([bool]$stallWaited)` tested nothing, because `Wait-Job` returns a job object and a job object is truthy even when its `State` is `Failed` — measured on a job whose body was `throw 'copy failed'` — so the drill now requires `State -eq 'Completed'` and requires the returned value to be the named refusal.
- **Seven corrections to SR-066, each re-measured from the committed tree rather than recalled.** (1) SR-066 states the backslash rule **backwards**: it says "`\\"` is a literal quote and not a delimiter." Measured, `node.exe a\\"b c` yields `node.exe`, `a\b c` — the even run makes the quote a **delimiter** — while the odd run `node.exe a\\\"b c` yields `node.exe`, `a\"b`, `c`, where the quote is literal. (2) SR-066 claims a "thirty-case equivalence table"; the table committed at `2321904` has **28** entries. (3) SR-066 says the static-guard diff added "nineteen" pins; `git diff 7dacc77 2321904` shows **20** added and 5 removed. (4) SR-066 says `-ErrorAction SilentlyContinue` "is still on all five calls in that path"; there are **four** in `Clear-MapleSeasonBrowserPort`. (5) SR-066 credits its three-case stall control as "reproducible from the commit by anyone who runs the regression"; the control was a scratch script and is not in the commit, so only the bounded drill is reproducible. (6) The 22-case predicate probe SR-066 cites as evidence was likewise never committed, so that measurement is unsupported by the artifact. (7) The claim about PID `22580` is **false again** — the same error SR-065 had already corrected once. Its command line is `"C:\Program Files\nodejs\node.exe" scripts/factory-board.mjs --port 4177`, which names our tree in no spelling at all, so it is not "the command line the fourth hole would have authorized killing," and no committed regression contained it as a case. Rather than only correct the prose, that exact command line is now a case in the new suite's portable refusal table, where what actually refuses it is verified: the script path is **relative**, and the predicate refuses to resolve a relative path at all rather than resolving it against wherever the shell happens to be standing.
- **Two of this round's own errors, both caught by running things rather than reading them.** The new tokenizer table initially argued for the bug it exists to refute: PowerShell's comma operator binds tighter than `+`, so `@('node.exe', 'C:\FarmRx'+[char]0x00A0+'Backup\server.js')` parsed as a **four**-element array, i.e. the table expected the tokenizer to split on a non-breaking space. Two of 29 cases failed against a correct implementation, which is how it surfaced; both concatenations are now parenthesized and the mis-parse is recorded in a comment. Separately, and worth recording because it bears on evidence integrity: after gutting the predicate to take the measurement above, I restored it with `git checkout --`, which reverted to `2321904` and destroyed all four uncommitted round-4 fixes. It was caught immediately from `git diff --stat` and recovered from a copy taken before the gutting, verified to carry all three markers; every subsequent sabotage measurement in this round restored from a file copy, never from git.
- **Executable evidence at this exact working tree, each run and watched.** `npx tsc -b --force` exited `0`. `node scripts/foundation-static-guards.mjs` → `Foundation static guards: PASS`. `node scripts/verify-foundation-mutations.mjs` → `Foundation mutation drill: PASS (91 controlled mutations turned the gate red)`, up from 74 at `2321904`. `scripts/maple-season-browser-port-preflight.regression.ps1` → `MAPLE_SEASON_BROWSER_PORT_PREFLIGHT_REGRESSION_PASS`. `scripts/maple-season-browser-ownership.regression.ps1` → `MAPLE_SEASON_BROWSER_OWNERSHIP_REGRESSION_PASS` under Windows PowerShell 5.1, under PowerShell 7.6.3, and under the forced non-Windows branch, which printed its skip line and exited `0`. Against a gutted predicate the same suite printed `FAIL 25 case(s)` on the non-Windows path and `FAIL 35 case(s)` on Windows. The full gate, `powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File scripts/verify-foundation.ps1`, was re-run after the last edit to any file and printed `Farm Rx foundation gate: PASS`, with the new lane's marker appearing in the gate's own output.
- **Honestly unfixed, and named rather than counted as done.** The cleanup path is untouched this round and is the next tranche: the force kill still goes through `$ownedProcess.Kill()` on a `Get-Process` object that stores a process **id**, not a handle, so a validated process that exits before the kill can have its number reissued and the kill can land on the reused pid — the correct repair is `OpenProcess` once, verify creation time via `GetProcessTimes` on that handle, then `TerminateProcess` on the same handle (F11); the whole listener set is not validated before any of it is mutated (F12/F13); two branches still continue silently and `WaitForExit`'s boolean still goes unchecked (F14); and there is no negative pin stopping `Stop-Process -Id` from reappearing in cleanup, nor a guard against a workflow step carrying `if: false`. The guard-integrity tranche is also open: the purity pin is case-sensitive and missing `$ExecutionContext`, `Get-Location`, `Resolve-Path`, `$env:` and the .NET filesystem APIs (F10), and the runtime drill's accounting slice takes the **first** occurrence of its anchor rather than requiring a unique one (F11/F12 of that file). Three classes remain unanswerable from a command-line string and are not string-solvable: a foreign process merely naming our root as a label (F6), a sibling worktree or a junction/symlink spelling of the same tree (F7), and case-sensitive directory names (F10). The **Job Object** proposal — `CreateJobObjectW`, `AssignProcessToJobObject`, `TerminateJobObject` — remains the only mechanism that expresses "everything this proof started, however deep," and it is a design change to the launch path of every season proof, so it remains Mason's decision. Also still open for Mason: wiring the nine orphaned `scripts/*.regression.ps1` files into a gate plus a blanket "every regression must have a caller" guard, which would turn the gate red today; and adding a Windows CI job, which narrows but does not close the forged-marker class. Weather-to-spray remains manual payload-free transcription with no provenance, integration, or automatic prefill.
- **Boundary:** all work was local. Nothing was pushed, opened as a pull request, merged, or deployed. No migration was applied to any database, and no database was touched beyond the disposable local project the gate's existing probes already use. No live data, Auth, permission, secret, customer account, or customer communication was touched, and `C:\CRX_Manager` was not modified. PID `22580` was verified running before and after this round's work, was never a target, and is now an executed must-answer-FALSE case rather than a prose assurance. This commit awaits its own fresh-context read-only Sol review and claims no acceptance. Farm Rx remains **RELEASE CANDIDATE READY**, not **COMPLETE**; both physical installed-PWA phone journeys remain open owner-operated gates documented in `docs/customer-zero-readiness-runbook.md`.

## SR-068 — Sol REQUEST-CHANGES on `f57e0d3` repaired: the completion marker measured forgeable and replaced by a challenge the caller holds, the two tokenizer tables unified under an enforced pairing, and six of SR-067's claims corrected

- **Date/time:** 2026-08-06 (`America/Chicago`).
- **Authority:** local repair work only. Mason's authorization covers local commits on worktree branch `claude/gauntlet-testing-sweep-013d65`. The earlier repair-plan approval question remains unanswered, the structural decisions raised in SR-062 through SR-067 remain unanswered, and no outward action was taken or is claimed as approved. This entry corrects SR-067 by appending; SR-060 through SR-067 are left exactly as written.
- **Origin:** a fresh-context read-only Sol review of the exact commit `f57e0d3` returned REQUEST-CHANGES with twenty-nine findings — ten BLOCKING, fourteen MAJOR, two MINOR, three NIT — including a truthfulness audit of SR-067. Fifteen are closed below and the other fourteen are named as open at the bottom rather than counted as done. Every finding acted on was reproduced on this workstation before any edit was made.
- **The blocking finding this round closes, and it is the same defect one layer up again.** SR-067's fix for "the guards pin text, not behaviour" was a suite whose completion marker both callers require. Sol's F11 observed that the marker is *also* just a string, and it was measured before it was believed: a file whose entire contents are `Write-Output 'MAPLE_SEASON_BROWSER_OWNERSHIP_REGRESSION_PASS'; exit 0` was accepted by **both** callers — the workflow step and the orchestrator lane — because each required only the marker plus a zero exit. Nothing the suite writes about itself can fix this, because the forger writes the log.
- **The fix is challenge/response, plus a manifest, and it is honest about which class it defeats.** The suite now prints `OWNERSHIP_MANIFEST tokenizer=29 refusals=25 gutted=25 windows=<bool> windowsCases=<n> challenges=<n>` and then answers command lines **supplied by its caller** through the REAL tokenizer and the REAL predicate, one `OWNERSHIP_CHALLENGE <i> owned=<TRUE|FALSE> argv=<vector>` line each. Both callers hold the expected answers longhand and require the exact manifest and each exact challenge line. This defeats a suite that has silently **stopped executing** — the accident that happens on edit. It does **not** defeat a deliberate forgery, because whoever forges the suite can also edit the caller that holds the answers; only kernel provenance removes command-line inference entirely, which is the Job Object decision still open. Row 1 of the challenge is the live unrelated `node.exe scripts/factory-board.mjs --port 4177` process that holds this port on this workstation, so the suite is now asked every run whether it would authorize killing it, and must answer FALSE.
- **A measurement that changed the design rather than confirming it: `-File` silently truncates array parameters.** The challenge began as `[string[]]$Challenge`. Measured across the two transports the two callers actually use: the orchestrator spawns the suite with `powershell.exe -File`, and through `-File` the array parameter bound only the **first** element and discarded the rest — exit `0`, no warning, three of four challenges never asked — while the workflow's in-process dot-call bound all four. A challenge nobody asks is worse than no challenge, because it reads as coverage. It became one `[string]` joined by `[char]0x1F` (U+001F), a character that cannot occur in a Windows command line, split inside the suite.
- **And then the joined string was eaten too, by the same transport, for a different reason — found by the challenge itself, in a full gate run.** With the U+001F join in place the gate went **red**, and the failure was real: challenge 1 came back as `OWNERSHIP_CHALLENGE 1 owned=FALSE argv=<C:\Program>` and challenges 2 and 3 were never asked at all, while the suite still printed its completion marker and exited `0`. The payload *is* command lines, which is the most hostile possible thing to put on a command line, and one row legitimately begins `"C:\Program Files\nodejs\node.exe"` — through `-File` the string was **truncated at that first double quote**. A delimiter no command line can contain does not help when the **quoting** is what breaks. The payload is now **Base64** on both encode sides and decoded inside the suite: its alphabet contains no space, no quote, no backslash and no metacharacter, so nothing is left for any shell, `-File` binder or YAML block scalar to reinterpret. Measured after the change: all four challenges arrive intact through `-File`, including the quoted row, with the exact argv vectors. Two lessons are recorded rather than one. First, **this is direct evidence the challenge design has teeth** — the defect was invisible to the marker, invisible to the exit code, and invisible to the manifest, and was caught only because the caller holds the expected argv longhand and the suite had to reproduce it. Second, "fewer challenges asked" was being caught *incidentally*, by an argv that happened to look wrong; the manifest now reports `challenges=<n>` decoded and both callers check it against the count they sent, so a payload lost in transit fails by name. Four new mutations hold the transport permanently: the suite reading the payload undecoded, and either caller dropping its Base64 encode or its challenge-count assertion.
- **F10 quantified, then closed by an enforced pairing rather than by prose.** SR-067 claimed the Windows socket regression re-derives the portable table's 29 hard-coded vectors from real `CommandLineToArgvW`. Measured by diffing the two tables: **29 portable, 32 live, exactly one portable vector never re-derived** — `node.exe C:\FarmRx\.. --port 4177` — plus four live-only vectors. That vector is now in the live table, and a static guard re-derives the pairing every run instead of trusting the sentence. **The guard's own first version was wrong in the direction that matters**, and it is recorded because it is the same class of defect: its pattern captured 26 rows while 29 begin the table, and the count was hard-coded at 27. Three rows contain a character no single-quoted PowerShell string can carry — a tab, a non-breaking space, a tab inside quotes — so they are built by expression. Rather than widen the count and move on, the guard now asserts the row total (29) **and** the literal total (26) separately, and the three excluded rows are enumerated by name with the spelling that must exist on **both** sides. Four new mutations prove each half: deleting the live tab vector, the live non-breaking-space vector, the portable quoted-tab row, or adding a portable row nothing re-derives, each turns the guard red with its own named label.
- **F9 closed by identity rather than by cardinality, including the defect Sol found inside it.** SR-067's anti-vacuity check required the refusal table to reject a gutted predicate on an **exact count**. Measured: `[string]$CommandLine` on the test-listener factory coerced `$null` to the empty string, so the "command line cannot be read" row and the "empty command line" row arrived at the predicate as the **same** zero-length String — 25 rows holding 24 distinct inputs, invisible to a count. The factory no longer types either parameter, so the null survives; the check now compares the **set of row labels** that caught the gutted predicate and names any that failed; and two further assertions require labels to be unique and require the 25 runtime inputs to be distinct, so a table of one case repeated 25 times fails instead of passing.
- **F12 closed structurally, and proven by three real disabling edits.** `if: false` on a governed workflow step satisfied every text pin while the step never ran. The guard now walks the workflow's step boundaries and rejects `if:` or `continue-on-error:` on any of the five governed steps, and requires the `foundation` job itself to be unconditional. Proven by planting each disabling edit in turn — `if: false` on the ownership step, `continue-on-error: true` on the gate step, `if: false` on the job — watching the guard go red each time, restoring from a file copy, and confirming no residue. Three mutations now hold those cases permanently.
- **A claim withdrawn rather than repaired, because the correct answer is unmeasured.** SR-067 and the suite both stated that off Windows "no Windows-shaped root can resolve inside itself," so every case answers FALSE. Reading the code rather than the comment: `Test-MapleSeasonArgumentIsInsideTree` decides the containment boundary with a hard-coded literal `'\'`, not `DirectorySeparatorChar`, and off Windows `GetFullPath` prefixes the current directory to the **argument and the root alike**, so the prefix comparison can still match. The mechanism therefore does not follow and **is withdrawn**. The correct off-Windows verdict for a genuinely-inside-the-tree path is **not measured**: there is no Linux .NET on this workstation, and settling it would mean downloading a PowerShell container image, which is an outward action nobody approved. Both callers now mark that one row `ResolverDependent`: off Windows they still require the exact argv vector and require an answer, and deliberately do not claim which verdict is right. The `C:\FarmRx2` sibling row is *not* resolver-dependent, and the reason is arithmetic rather than a resolver: whatever a platform prepends, it prepends to both sides, so the character after the root text is still `2` and never a separator.
- **Six corrections to SR-067, each re-measured rather than recalled.** (1) The **"85 mutations"** figure is chronologically impossible against the commits: parent `2321904` holds 74 drills and `f57e0d3` holds 91, so 85 was measured on an intermediate **uncommitted** tree and is reproducible from no commit. Withdrawn; no substitute number is offered, because the honest statement is that the measurement's tree no longer exists. (2) The claim that an exact count "proves the whole table reachable and not merely entered" was **false** for the reason in F9 above. (3) The claim that the socket regression "still re-derives [the same 29] live" was **false by exactly one vector**, now named and quantified. (4) The claim that Windows' two argument grammars "disagree on exactly one construct" and that there is "exactly one place" deciding quote semantics is **withdrawn**: the odd-backslash branch still consumes a quote of its own. (5) **The claim that `WaitForExit`'s boolean "still goes unchecked" was my own error, and it was false.** It is checked at `scripts/maple-season-browser.ps1:328` and throws on `false`; the process-id kill window is also already narrowed by taking the process object before the check and comparing creation time to start time. That open item is removed. (6) Several measurements SR-067 credits — the prior review result, the sliced orchestrator controls, the parsed-YAML experiment, the checkout-and-recovery incident, and the PID-before-and-after observation — are **not reproducible from the repository alone**, and are hereby labelled workstation observations rather than artifacts.
- **One of Sol's own mechanisms refuted by measurement, with its conclusion left standing.** Sol's F2 said the tokenizer's `argv[0]` handling disagrees with the Microsoft C runtime that Node uses, and gave `node.exe"C:\Other C:\FarmRx\safe" --port 4177` as a demonstration. Measured against real `CommandLineToArgvW` across seven `argv[0]` constructs including that one, the committed tokenizer **agrees on all seven**, so the disagreement as stated does not exist. The **conclusion** is nonetheless real and remains open: that command line does return TRUE, by way of F1's rule that *any* argument inside the tree establishes ownership, not by way of a grammar disagreement.
- **Three stale or wrong comments corrected (F25, F26, F27, F28) and one rename (F29).** Six comment sites across four files described the force kill as `Stop-Process -Force`; the shipping kill is `$ownedProcess.Kill()` on a process object taken before the check, and the comments now say so and name the identity risk that remains. The comment claiming the gutted predicate "fails 24 of these cases on Linux" is corrected to state that all 25 rows claim it. The "four drills below" comment is corrected. The comment claiming every portable refusal "returns before the path resolver is reached" was an overclaim — the sibling row reaches the resolver, and off Windows several rows are refused by a different branch than on Windows — and is replaced by an explicit three-group classification that claims only what the table asserts: the same verdict in both worlds, not the same branch. `Split-MapleSeasonCommandLineArgument` is renamed to `Split-MapleSeasonCommandLineArguments` across all five files that reference it, since it returns a vector; SR-066's use of the old name is left exactly as written, as an append-only record of what the function was called then.
- **Executable evidence at this exact working tree, each run and watched, all of it postdating the last edit to any file.** `npx tsc -b --force` exited `0`. `node scripts/foundation-static-guards.mjs` → `Foundation static guards: PASS`. `node scripts/verify-foundation-mutations.mjs` → `Foundation mutation drill: PASS (113 controlled mutations turned the gate red)`, up from 91 at `f57e0d3`, with every mutation detected. `scripts/maple-season-browser-ownership.regression.ps1`, spawned through `powershell.exe -File` with a Base64 challenge — the exact transport that ate the payload twice — printed `OWNERSHIP_MANIFEST tokenizer=29 refusals=25 gutted=25 windows=true windowsCases=24 challenges=4`, answered all four challenges with their exact argv vectors (`0 owned=TRUE`, `1 owned=FALSE argv=<C:\Program Files\nodejs\node.exe>|<scripts/factory-board.mjs>|<--port>|<4177>`, `2 owned=FALSE`, `3 owned=FALSE`), printed `MAPLE_SEASON_BROWSER_OWNERSHIP_REGRESSION_PASS`, and exited `0`. The workflow's ownership step body, extracted from `.github/workflows/foundation.yml` and run locally, produced the identical four answers and exited `0`, so **both** callers were watched working rather than one. The full `scripts/verify-foundation.ps1` gate then ran end to end: `GATE_EXIT=0`, 1,266 lines, with the previously-red ownership lane green at line 283 (`challenges=4`) and `Farm Rx foundation gate: PASS` at line 1,266. Every disposable proof, the RLS role matrix, and the built-browser suite ran inside that pass. The prior run of this same gate is what returned `GATE_EXIT=1` on the truncated challenge, so the red-to-green transition was observed, not inferred.
- **Honestly unfixed, and named rather than counted as done.** Fourteen of Sol's twenty-nine findings are open. The kill-authorizing predicate still infers ownership from a command-line string, and that is the root of five of them: **any** argument inside the tree establishes ownership (F1), lexical containment does not resolve junctions or symlinks (F3), `OrdinalIgnoreCase` authorizes case-different paths where Windows per-directory case sensitivity makes them distinct (F4), equivalent spellings via `subst`/8.3/UNC answer FALSE and produce the expensive wrong-diagnosis failure (F5), and a command line truncated by its producing API can leave a root-valued prefix that authorizes a kill (F6). F2's conclusion stands as above, and F7's blanket refusal of `""` still rejects valid Node command lines. The cleanup path is untouched this round and remains the next tranche: `Get-Process` yields a process **id**, not a pinned handle (F13), the listener snapshot can go stale and the ±1-second creation-time comparison accepts a replacement born in the same window (F14), listeners are validated and killed sequentially so a destructive partial mutation can precede the throw (F15), the timeout path still calls `taskkill /PID` (F16), `Get-NetTCPConnection -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue` makes a query failure indistinguishable from "no listener" on both calls (F17), and the guard treats `.Kill()` as evidence of identity-safe termination when it is not (F18). F8 is unfixed by design this round: `ubuntu-latest` skips every must-be-TRUE assertion, so an always-FALSE predicate still passes CI, and the fix is a **Windows CI job** — which would also settle the off-Windows verdict withdrawn above. F24 is accepted rather than fixed: this ledger's workstation observations are not reproducible from the repository, and committing sanitized evidence tied to tree hashes is a process change, not an edit. The **Job Object** proposal — `CreateJobObjectW`, `AssignProcessToJobObject`, `TerminateJobObject` — remains the only mechanism that expresses "everything this proof started, however deep," and it is a design change to the launch path of every season proof, so it and F1's ownership semantics remain Mason's decision and are to be presented together. Also still open for Mason: wiring the nine orphaned `scripts/*.regression.ps1` files into a gate plus a blanket "every regression must have a caller" guard, which would turn the gate red today. Weather-to-spray remains manual payload-free transcription with no provenance, integration, or automatic prefill.
- **Boundary:** all work was local. Nothing was pushed, opened as a pull request, merged, or deployed. No migration was applied to any database, and no database was touched beyond the disposable local project the gate's existing probes already use. No live data, Auth, permission, secret, customer account, or customer communication was touched, and `C:\CRX_Manager` was not modified. No file was downloaded, including the PowerShell container image that would have settled the off-Windows verdict. The live `node.exe scripts/factory-board.mjs --port 4177` process was never a target and is an executed must-answer-FALSE challenge row in both callers. Every sabotage measurement in this round was restored from a file copy, never from git, following SR-067's recorded incident. This commit awaits its own fresh-context read-only Sol review and claims no acceptance. Farm Rx remains **RELEASE CANDIDATE READY**, not **COMPLETE**; both physical installed-PWA phone journeys remain open owner-operated gates documented in `docs/customer-zero-readiness-runbook.md`.

## SR-069 — Sol REQUEST-CHANGES on `726f48c` repaired: the assertion helper measured able to disable ~100 checks silently, the pairing guard defeated by a commented-out row, the job guard defeated by moving one line, and four of SR-068's claims corrected

- **Date/time:** 2026-08-06 (`America/Chicago`).
- **Authority:** local repair work only. Mason's authorization covers local commits on worktree branch `claude/gauntlet-testing-sweep-013d65`. The earlier repair-plan approval question remains unanswered, the structural decisions raised in SR-062 through SR-068 remain unanswered, and no outward action was taken or is claimed as approved. This entry corrects SR-068 by appending; SR-060 through SR-068 are left exactly as written.
- **Origin:** a fresh-context read-only Sol review of the exact commit `726f48c` returned REQUEST-CHANGES with thirteen findings, including a truthfulness audit of SR-068. Four were structural defects in the guards SR-068 had just added; each was **reproduced on this workstation before any repair was written**, because Sol's stated mechanisms have been wrong before in this same tranche. The predicate and cleanup-path findings are named as open at the bottom rather than counted as done.
- **The defect is at layer four, and it is the same defect.** The chain is now on the record: substring pins proved text, not behaviour (SR-067); the behavioural suite that replaced them was gated on a completion **marker**, which is also just a string (SR-068); the challenge/response that replaced the marker was defeated by the **transport** carrying it (SR-068, twice); and this round the challenge survives while the suite's **assertion helper** does not.
- **The blocking finding, measured before it was believed.** `Assert-True` in the ownership suite was changed to `if ($false) { $script:failures += $Message }` — one edit, four characters. The suite then: printed its completion marker, published `OWNERSHIP_MANIFEST` with **every table at full declared size**, answered **all four** caller challenges correctly with exact argv vectors from the real tokenizer and the real predicate, and exited `0` — with roughly **one hundred table assertions dead**. Neither caller noticed, and neither could: the manifest counts table **sizes**, which are independent of whether their assertions do anything, and the challenge answers are computed **outside** the helper. Both gates were green on a suite that checked nothing.
- **The fix is a canary the helper cannot pass without working, plus a static pin, plus a caller-visible field.** The suite now hands `Assert-True` a deliberately-false condition **before any real assertion runs** and `throw`s — not asserts, because the helper is the thing on trial — if the failure was not recorded, refusing to report a result at all. It then removes the canary from the failure list, verifies nothing else was left behind, and sets `canary=caught`, which is published in the manifest and **required by both callers**. A static guard pins the helper's body, the canary block, and the terminating `throw`. Five new mutations hold it: no-op'ing the helper, deleting the canary, downgrading its `throw` to `Write-Output`, publishing `skipped` instead of `caught`, and dropping the manifest field.
- **SR-068's claim that the two tokenizer tables were "unified under an enforced pairing" is corrected: the pairing was a substring search, and it was defeated by a comment character.** The guard tested `preflightRegression.includes("'<literal>'")` — an unrestricted search over the whole live file. Measured: **commenting out** a live row stopped it executing while leaving its text in the file, and the guard stayed **green**. The live table is now **parsed** into its actual rows (sliced between the `foreach` header and its closing `)) {`, comment lines excluded by construction) and the pairing is asserted in **both directions**: 33 live rows = 26 single-quoted portable literals + 3 expression-built hand-paired rows + 4 live-only rows, each of the four named, and any live row belonging to none of those groups fails with its own text in the label. Proven by planting both sabotages: commenting a row produced `tokenizer-literal-rederived:node.exe C:\FarmRx\x.js` and a row-count failure; adding an unasserted live row produced `live-row-unaccounted:'node.exe C:\FarmRx\unasserted.js'`. Four new mutations hold each direction.
- **SR-068's claim that "F12 closed structurally" is corrected: the job-level half was adjacency, and moving one line defeated it.** The guard required `foundation:` to be followed **immediately** by `runs-on:`, reasoning that a condition would have to be inserted between them. Measured: `if: false` on the line **after** `runs-on:` is valid YAML, disables all five governed steps at once, and the guard stayed green — and the existing mutation drill only ever inserted the condition **before** `runs-on`, so it was green too. Adjacency was never the property worth asserting. The guard now scans the whole `foundation` job block by indentation — job keys at four spaces, step keys at eight — and rejects `if:` or `continue-on-error:` anywhere at job level, with the declaration check kept separately. A new mutation inserts the condition in the direction that was missed.
- **SR-068's claim that the challenge "defeats a suite that has silently stopped executing" was too strong, and is corrected.** It defeats a suite that has stopped **printing** and a suite whose tables have shrunk. It did **not** defeat a suite whose assertion helper had stopped asserting, which is the most likely accident of all, since a helper is one line and a hundred call sites depend on it. With the canary in place the claim now holds for the helper too, and the honest boundary is unchanged: none of this defeats a deliberate forgery, because the forger writes the caller.
- **A caller check that measured nothing, and the fix.** Both callers built their expected `OWNERSHIP_CHALLENGE` strings and then filtered **their own candidates** by whether the output contained them, requiring exactly one match. That counts candidates, not output: a child printing **both** `owned=TRUE` and `owned=FALSE` for the same index, or the same answer twice, satisfied it. Both callers now select actual output **instances** with an ordinal `StartsWith`, require the total number of `OWNERSHIP_CHALLENGE` lines to equal the number of challenges sent, require exactly one line per index, and require that line to be an accepted spelling. A child that contradicts itself has not answered. Four new mutations per caller hold it.
- **The fixed challenge rows were hard-codeable, so two of them are now different on every run.** Four static rows can be satisfied by a stub that returns the right strings without ever calling the predicate. Both callers now append two rows carrying a per-run nonce — one that must answer TRUE and one that must answer FALSE — so the child has to tokenize and judge text it has never seen. The nonce is hexadecimal only, so the expected argv is plain concatenation and neither caller re-implements the tokenizer. The determinism trade-off is deliberate and narrow, and recorded here rather than left implicit: the season proof's fixed `America/Chicago` 2027 clock, deterministic UUIDs and synthetic fixtures are untouched, because the nonce exists only inside this challenge and reaches no product code, no database and no artifact. Six new mutations hold both rows and the nonce's freshness in both callers.
- **A claim of impossibility replaced by a check, after measuring that it was false.** SR-068 and three comment sites stated that U+001F, the delimiter joining the challenge payload, "cannot occur in a Windows command line." Measured: `A<U+001F>B` passed to a Node child arrives as a **three-character** argument whose middle code point is 31. The claim is **withdrawn**. Base64 makes it moot for today's rows, but a row containing the delimiter would still split into phantom challenges, so both callers now **refuse** to send such a row rather than asserting it cannot exist. The guard pins the refusal as a live statement (anchored, so a commented-out refusal fails), and a mutation neuters it.
- **Two further withdrawn claims corrected at their remaining sites.** The statement that Windows' two argument grammars "disagree about **exactly one** construct" — already withdrawn in SR-068 — still stood in `scripts/maple-season-browser.ps1` and `scripts/maple-season-browser-port-preflight.regression.ps1`; both now claim only the disagreement that was measured (the doubled quote), not a count of all disagreements. The workflow's comment claiming all three must-be-FALSE rows are "refused before the platform path resolver is consulted" is corrected: exactly one of them is (the doubled-quote row); the other two are FALSE by prefix arithmetic, and their off-Windows verdict is an **observation** — the job runs on ubuntu and asserts them — not an argument. The suite's own off-Windows skip message likewise now says only that those cases are not run here.
- **Executable evidence at this exact working tree, each run and watched, all of it postdating the last edit to any file.** `npx tsc -b --force` exited `0`. `node scripts/foundation-static-guards.mjs` → `Foundation static guards: PASS`. `node scripts/verify-foundation-mutations.mjs` → `Foundation mutation drill: PASS (139 controlled mutations turned the gate red)`, up from 113 at `726f48c`, with every mutation detected and the harness still refusing a stale needle. `scripts/maple-season-browser-ownership.regression.ps1`, spawned through `powershell.exe -File` with the Base64 challenge — the transport that ate the payload twice — printed `OWNERSHIP_MANIFEST tokenizer=29 refusals=25 gutted=25 windows=true windowsCases=24 challenges=6 canary=caught` and answered all **six** challenges with exact argv vectors, including both nonce rows (`4 owned=TRUE ... --nonce <fresh>`, `5 owned=FALSE ... --nonce <fresh>`), then exited `0`. The workflow's ownership step body, extracted from `.github/workflows/foundation.yml` and run locally, produced the same six answers under its own assertions and exited `0`, so **both** callers were watched working. The **whole** foundation gate was then run end to end from this working tree — `scripts/verify-foundation.ps1`, not just the changed lanes — and exited `0`, printing `Farm Rx foundation gate: PASS` as line **1294 of its 1294-line log**, with `Foundation mutation drill: PASS (139 controlled mutations turned the gate red)` at line 307, `Foundation Windows lane runtime drill: PASS (6 of 6 cases executed and validated on win32)` at line 308, the orchestrator's own ownership lane green at lines **309-316** carrying a nonce (`d913a254d9334c2d8ce19db27573e13c`) that appears nowhere in the repository and differed from every other run, and Playwright closing `62 passed, 14 skipped`. **The canary was then proven to catch the exact edit that defeated the previous design**: with `Assert-True`'s condition changed back to `if ($false)`, the suite threw `Assertion helper did not record a deliberately-false assertion, so every other check in this file is inert. Refusing to report a result.` and exited `1`, printing **no** marker and **no** manifest — where the same edit had produced a full green PASS an hour earlier. Restored from a file copy and re-run green. Each of the sabotage measurements in this entry was planted, watched going red, and restored **from a file copy, never from git**, with `git status --porcelain` confirmed clean after each.
- **Honestly unfixed, and named rather than counted as done.** The kill-authorizing predicate still infers ownership from a command-line string, and that is the root of the findings left open: **any** argument inside the tree establishes ownership (F1), lexical containment resolves neither junctions nor symlinks and `OrdinalIgnoreCase` authorizes case-different paths (F3, F4), a command line truncated by its producing API can leave a root-valued prefix that authorizes a kill (F6), and equivalent spellings via `subst`/8.3/UNC plus the blanket refusal of `""` produce false FALSEs and the expensive wrong-diagnosis failure (F5, F7). The cleanup path is untouched again this round and remains the next tranche: `Get-Process` yields a process **id**, not a pinned handle (F13), the listener snapshot can go stale and the ±1-second creation-time comparison accepts a replacement born in that window (F14), listeners are validated and killed **sequentially** so an owned listener can already be dead when a later foreign one throws (F15), the timeout path still calls `taskkill /PID` (F16), `Get-NetTCPConnection -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue` makes a query failure indistinguishable from "no listener" on both calls (F17), and the guard treats `.Kill()` as evidence of identity-safe termination when it is not (F18). F8 is unfixed by design: `ubuntu-latest` skips every must-be-TRUE assertion, so an always-FALSE predicate still passes CI, and the fix is a **Windows CI job** — which would also settle the off-Windows verdict withdrawn in SR-068. F24 is accepted rather than fixed: this ledger's workstation observations are not reproducible from the repository alone. The **Job Object** proposal — `CreateJobObjectW`, `AssignProcessToJobObject`, `TerminateJobObject` — remains the only mechanism that expresses "everything this proof started, however deep," and it is a design change to the launch path of every season proof, so it and F1's ownership semantics remain Mason's decision and are to be presented together. Also still open for Mason: wiring the nine orphaned `scripts/*.regression.ps1` files into a gate plus a blanket "every regression must have a caller" guard, which would turn the gate red today. Weather-to-spray remains manual payload-free transcription with no provenance, integration, or automatic prefill.
- **Boundary:** all work was local. Nothing was pushed, opened as a pull request, merged, or deployed. No migration was applied to any database, and no database was touched beyond the disposable local project the gate's existing probes already use. No live data, Auth, permission, secret, customer account, or customer communication was touched, and `C:\CRX_Manager` was not modified. No file was downloaded. The live `node.exe scripts/factory-board.mjs --port 4177` process was never a target and remains an executed must-answer-FALSE challenge row in both callers. This commit awaits its own fresh-context read-only Sol review and claims no acceptance. Farm Rx remains **RELEASE CANDIDATE READY**, not **COMPLETE**; both physical installed-PWA phone journeys remain open owner-operated gates documented in `docs/customer-zero-readiness-runbook.md`.

## SR-070 — Sol REQUEST-CHANGES on `bee7b6f` repaired: SR-069's canary disproved by a helper that honours only the canary, three guards defeated by wrapping the code they pin in `if ($false)`, a mirrored duplicate row that survived a Set-based pairing, and six of SR-069's claims corrected

- **Date/time:** 2026-08-06 (`America/Chicago`).
- **Authority:** local repair work only. Mason's authorization covers local commits on worktree branch `claude/gauntlet-testing-sweep-013d65`. The earlier repair-plan approval question remains unanswered, the structural decisions raised in SR-062 through SR-069 remain unanswered, and no outward action was taken or is claimed as approved. This entry corrects SR-069 by appending; SR-060 through SR-069 are left exactly as written.
- **Origin:** a fresh-context read-only Sol review of the exact commit `bee7b6f` returned REQUEST-CHANGES. Six findings were structural defects in the guards SR-069 had just added. **Every one was reproduced on this workstation before any repair was written**, and one of Sol's stated mechanisms turned out to be wrong — recorded below rather than quietly repaired — because Sol's mechanisms have been wrong before in this same tranche.
- **The defect is at layer five, and it now has a name: a static guard proves a statement EXISTS, not that it EXECUTES.** The chain on the record: substring pins proved text, not behaviour (SR-067); the behavioural suite that replaced them was gated on a completion **marker**, itself a string (SR-068); the challenge that replaced the marker was defeated by the **transport** carrying it (SR-068, twice); the suite's **assertion helper** could accept a false condition while every caller stayed green (SR-069); and this round three separate guards were defeated by leaving every pinned string in place and wrapping the code around it in `if ($false) { ... }`.
- **SR-069's central claim is disproved and withdrawn.** SR-069 called its fix "a canary the helper cannot pass without working." Sol wrote the helper that does exactly that: `if ($Message -ceq $script:assertionCanary) { $script:failures += $Message; return }`. The canary is recorded, `canary=caught` is published, the manifest is full-sized, all six challenges answer correctly, the marker prints, exit `0` — and every one of the file's real assertions is dead. **Reproduced.** A self-test whose input the subject can recognise is not a self-test, and no third canary fixes it, because whatever the suite hands its own helper the helper can be written to spot.
- **The fix is two independent reporting channels cross-checked by a `throw`, plus a drill that breaks the subject.** The suite's nine real call sites now go through `Assert-MapleSeasonCase`, a wrapper that keeps its own `$script:tallied` list and then **calls** `Assert-True` rather than **being** it. Before the manifest, the answers, or the marker are printed, the two counts are compared and a mismatch `throw`s — a `throw`, because a sabotaged helper can swallow an assertion but cannot swallow that. A sabotaged run therefore prints no manifest, no answers and no PASS. The static guard pins the wrapper at both retargeted call sites, which is what stops the two channels collapsing back into one.
- **And a drill that measures execution instead of text.** `scripts/verify-foundation-mutations.mjs` gains a **behavioural** half: it breaks the *subject* on disk and requires the suite that runs against it to say so **by name**, refusing to score a crash as a detection. Four broken subjects — the predicate authorizing everything, the predicate refusing everything, the tokenizer collapsing every command line into one argument, and Sol's canary-only helper combined with an authorize-everything predicate — plus a load-bearing baseline requiring the suite green on unmutated source. The drill first ran against the **unfixed** suite and failed, correctly; the two-channel repair is what turned it green. Its mutations INSERT beneath the suite's own gutting needle rather than replacing it, because replacing that line makes the suite throw instead of report, which the drill would otherwise have scored as a pass.
- **Three guards defeated by `if ($false)`, and the receipts that close them.** (1) The orchestrator's ownership lane: wrapping the answer-total check and the per-index loop left every pinned string present, the lane green, and not one of the six answers verified. Both callers now count the answers they actually verified and consume the count after the loop, with the same refusal sentence pinned in both. (2) The tokenizer equivalence table: wrapping its `foreach` left all 33 rows present, all pairing checks green, and zero command lines handed to `CommandLineToArgvW`. It now publishes `TOKENIZER_RECEIPT comparisons=… distinct=… windows=…`, and the counter is **declared before the Windows branch and consumed after it closes**, so wrapping the branch body cannot take the check with it. (3) The receipt is held by its **caller** as well: `scripts/maple-july-db-clock-wiring.regression.ps1` states `comparisons=33 distinct=33 windows=true` longhand, so deleting the child's own two assertions is not enough — a child asserting things about itself is the shape of defeat this chain keeps finding.
- **Sol's mechanism for the duplicated row was wrong, and the correct one is worse.** Sol claimed a duplicate row defeats the pairing. Measured: a **one-sided** duplicate is already caught — the displaced row goes unaccounted (`live-row-unaccounted:'node.exe   C:\FarmRx\x.js   --port   4177'`). Only a **MIRRORED** duplicate works, overwritten identically on both sides: array `.length` counts duplicates while `Set` collapses them, so every total held, every pairing resolved, and one real command line silently stopped being tested. Both tables now require their distinct count to equal their total, and the runtime receipt requires 33 distinct command lines as well as 33 comparisons — two channels, deliberately.
- **The YAML guard read one spelling of a key.** SR-069 claimed the whole `foundation` job is scanned. It scanned only **bare** keys: `'if': failure()` is the same YAML, disabled a governed step, and the guard stayed green. Keys are now normalized through one parser that accepts single-quoted, double-quoted and bare spellings at job indent and step indent alike.
- **The per-run nonce could be assigned live and then overwritten.** SR-069's guard pinned `[Guid]::NewGuid().ToString('N')`, which a second assignment leaves untouched: `$nonce = 'deadbeef…'` on the next line restores the fully hard-codeable challenge SR-069 said the nonce had ended. Both callers' nonce variables must now be assigned **exactly once**.
- **Two further SR-069 claims corrected.** Its description of the live table as parsed into "actual rows" is accurate about text and was read as a statement about execution, which it never was — hence the receipt above. Its claim that the nonce "reaches no product code, no database and no artifact" still holds and is re-verified; its claim that the nonce made the challenge un-hard-codeable did not, and is corrected by the single-assignment rule. Separately, **Sol's own review contained a measured error**: its claim about the live process holding port 4177 did not match this workstation, where the port had `rows=1` held by pid 22580, an unrelated `node.exe scripts/factory-board.mjs` that was never a target and remains an executed must-answer-FALSE challenge row in both callers.
- **The ownership lane was deliberately NOT extracted into a shared file.** The guard's own comment records why the two callers hold their expectations longhand: a challenge whose answers live in one shared place is one edit away from being no challenge at all. The counter closes the accident; the residual is stated below rather than papered over.
- **Executable evidence at this exact working tree, each run and watched, all of it postdating the last edit to any file.** `npx tsc -b --force` exited `0`. `node scripts/foundation-static-guards.mjs` → `Foundation static guards: PASS`. `scripts/maple-season-browser-port-preflight.regression.ps1` standalone printed `TOKENIZER_RECEIPT comparisons=33 distinct=33 windows=true` then its PASS marker and exited `0`. The **whole** foundation gate was run end to end from this working tree — `scripts/verify-foundation.ps1`, not just the changed lanes — and exited `0` in 6m20s, printing `Farm Rx foundation gate: PASS` as line **1299 of its 1299-line log**, with `Foundation mutation drill: PASS (139 controlled mutations turned the gate red)` at line 307, `Foundation behavioural mutation drill: PASS (4 broken subjects were reported by the suite that runs against them)` at line 312, `OWNERSHIP_MANIFEST tokenizer=29 refusals=25 gutted=25 windows=true windowsCases=24 challenges=6 canary=caught` at line 314, all six challenge answers at lines 315-320 carrying a nonce (`76adbd7b270d445f84a23c43ecd95cb0`) that appears nowhere in the repository, and Playwright closing `62 passed, 14 skipped`.
- **Every repair was proven by planting the defeat it exists to catch and watching the gate go red.** The tokenizer table wrapped in `if ($false)` → `TOKENIZER_RECEIPT comparisons=0 distinct=0 windows=true` and `REGRESSION_FAIL The tokenizer equivalence table handed 0 command lines to CommandLineToArgvW instead of 33`, exit `1`. The mirrored duplicate → `tokenizer-literal-distinct-count` and `live-tokenizer-distinct-row-count`, with the one-sided control still failing as `live-row-unaccounted`. The quoted step gate → `workflow:governed-step-unconditional:Foundation mutation drill`. The overwritten nonce → `orchestrator:ownership-challenge-nonce-assigned-once`. The orchestrator's ownership loop wrapped in `if ($false)` → **static guards stayed green, which is the point**, and the gate then exited `1` with `6 challenge answers this lane did not verify one by one`. Each sabotage was restored **from a file copy, never from git** — an earlier `git checkout --` on one of these files destroyed four uncommitted fixes — with `git status --porcelain` confirmed after each.
- **Running the gate found a defect the tests could not.** Publishing the receipt broke `maple-july-db-clock-wiring.regression.ps1`, which requires the child's entire output to equal its PASS marker exactly. That is why the caller now holds the receipt expectation instead of the line merely being appended, and why the mutation drill's baseline file list gained that caller. Neither would have surfaced from unit checks; the gate had to be run.
- **Residual limits, named rather than counted as done.** A counter proves a skipped block only while the counter's own check runs: an author who wraps the check *too* defeats it, and in `scripts/verify-foundation.ps1` the counter's declaration sits **inside** the region a reviewer would wrap — it still fired in the drill above, because an unset variable compares unequal to six, and the measured refusal read `only  were checked` with the count rendered empty. That is fail-closed by accident, not by design, and is the honest boundary. Nothing here defeats a deliberate forgery, because the forger writes the caller.
- **Honestly unfixed, and carried unchanged from SR-069.** The kill-authorizing predicate still infers ownership from a command-line string: **any** argument inside the tree establishes ownership (F1), lexical containment resolves neither junctions nor symlinks and `OrdinalIgnoreCase` authorizes case-different paths (F3, F4), a truncated command line can leave a root-valued prefix that authorizes a kill (F6), and `subst`/8.3/UNC aliases plus the blanket refusal of `""` produce false FALSEs and the expensive wrong-diagnosis failure (F5, F7). **The cleanup path is untouched for a third round and is the next tranche — and it is the actual workstation-safety defect, higher value than a sixth layer of guard-the-guard**: `Get-Process` yields a process **id**, not a pinned handle (F13), the ±1-second creation-time comparison accepts a replacement born in that window (F14, measured across 304 processes: max 9 FILETIME ticks, median 4, only 34 exactly equal, so a 10-tick check is both tighter and safe), listeners are validated and killed **sequentially** so an owned listener can already be dead when a later foreign one throws (F15), the timeout path still calls `taskkill /PID` (F16), `Get-NetTCPConnection -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue` makes a query failure indistinguishable from "no listener" (F17), and the guard treats `.Kill()` as evidence of identity-safe termination when it is not (F18). F8 is unfixed by design: `ubuntu-latest` skips every must-be-TRUE assertion, so an always-FALSE predicate still passes CI, and the fix is a **Windows CI job**. F24 is accepted rather than fixed: this ledger's workstation observations are not reproducible from the repository alone. The **Job Object** proposal — `CreateJobObjectW`, `AssignProcessToJobObject`, `TerminateJobObject` — remains the only mechanism that expresses "everything this proof started, however deep," is a design change to the launch path of every season proof, and remains Mason's decision to be presented together with F1's ownership semantics. Also still open for Mason: the Windows CI job, and wiring the nine orphaned `scripts/*.regression.ps1` files into a gate plus a blanket "every regression must have a caller" guard, which would turn the gate red today. Weather-to-spray remains manual payload-free transcription with no provenance, integration, or automatic prefill.
- **Boundary:** all work was local. Nothing was pushed, opened as a pull request, merged, or deployed. No migration was applied to any database, and no database was touched beyond the disposable local project the gate's existing probes already use. No live data, Auth, permission, secret, customer account, or customer communication was touched, and `C:\CRX_Manager` was not modified. No file was downloaded. The live `node.exe scripts/factory-board.mjs --port 4177` process was never a target. This commit awaits its own fresh-context read-only Sol review and claims no acceptance. Farm Rx remains **RELEASE CANDIDATE READY**, not **COMPLETE**; both physical installed-PWA phone journeys remain open owner-operated gates documented in `docs/customer-zero-readiness-runbook.md`.

## SR-071 — Sol REQUEST-CHANGES on `17ddad8` repaired: SR-070's counters measured to prove nothing because they counted before the thing they measured, the ubuntu lane's blind spot measured to be a CI break rather than a residual, and four of SR-070's claims corrected

- **Date/time:** 2026-08-06 (`America/Chicago`).
- **Authority:** local repair work only. Mason's authorization covers local commits on worktree branch `claude/gauntlet-testing-sweep-013d65`. The earlier repair-plan approval question remains unanswered, the structural decisions raised in SR-062 through SR-070 remain unanswered, and no outward action was taken or is claimed as approved. This entry corrects SR-070 by appending; SR-060 through SR-070 are left exactly as written.
- **Origin:** a fresh-context read-only Sol review of the exact commit `17ddad8` returned REQUEST-CHANGES with thirteen findings. Nine are closed below; four are named as open or residual at the bottom rather than counted as done. **The three most serious were reproduced on this workstation before any repair was written**, because Sol's stated mechanism has been wrong before in this same tranche.
- **The defect is layer six, and it has a name too: a counter placed before or outside the thing it measures proves nothing.** SR-070's answer to "a static guard proves a statement EXISTS, not that it EXECUTES" was to count executions and consume the count. Sol defeated both counters SR-070 added, by the same move each time — leave the counter where it is and wrap only the work.
- **Finding 6, reproduced and confirmed.** `$tokenizerComparisons++` sat *before* the comparison it claimed to measure. Wrapping the comparison block in `if ($false) { … }` left the static guard green, the preflight regression exiting `0`, its PASS marker printed, and the receipt reading `TOKENIZER_RECEIPT comparisons=33 distinct=33 windows=true` — with **zero** command lines handed to `CommandLineToArgvW`. The repair clears `$agrees` to `$null` *first*, so a stale agreement from the previous row cannot authorize the receipt, and records the comparison only inside `if ($agrees) { … }` — a value that exists only because the comparison ran.
- **Finding 5, reproduced and confirmed.** SR-070's "two independent reporting channels" were both failure-only. Wrapping an assertion that normally **passes** left both channels at `0`, agreeing, with the suite exiting `0`, printing PASS, and publishing a full manifest. The repair tallies **every** case unconditionally, publishes `cases=` in the manifest, and derives the expected total (`5 + $windowsCasesRun`) — compared with `-lt`, not `-ne`, because two call sites live inside failure loops and a `-ne` would have replaced a real defect report with arithmetic about tallies. Both callers hold the expected count independently, so the number lives where the suite cannot edit it.
- **Finding 1 was not a residual — it was a CI break, and it was measured as one.** SR-070 recorded the ubuntu blind spot (F8) as "unfixed by design." Sol observed that SR-070's own behavioural drill contains a `refuses everything` case, `.github/workflows/foundation.yml` runs on `ubuntu-latest`, and on that runner the suite skips every must-be-TRUE assertion. Reproduced: with `$onWindows` forced false and the predicate returning `$false`, the suite exits `0` with PASS and `windowsCases=0` — so the drill throws, on the only platform where CI runs it. The repair does not pretend to fix the platform: where a platform cannot see a defect, the drill now asserts the **blind spot** and prints `BEHAVIOUR_GAP` via `unseenByBehaviour(label, why)`, and each caller holds a platform-specific count — the workflow requires `(4 broken subjects …, 1 not measurable on this platform)`, the orchestrator requires `(5 …, 0 …)`. A gap that is measured and named is not the same as a gap that is skipped silently.
- **Finding 8: bare-tick counters replaced by value-carrying reconciliation.** SR-070's `$verifiedAnswers++` sat on its own line after the per-index comparison, so wrapping only the comparison left the tick firing once per row and the count reaching the expected total with nothing compared. Both callers now select the accepted answer by filtering their **own** candidate spellings, record `$accepted[0]` — a value the comparison produced — and reconcile the recorded answers against what the child printed. A skipped comparison leaves an empty or null-filled list that cannot reconcile.
- **Finding 2: the drill's behavioural half is now held by its callers.** The drill decided for itself what it had covered, so wrapping the behavioural half left it printing PASS. Each caller now states the exact behavioural claim it requires and refuses when the sentence is absent — which is why the two callers hold different sentences, and why neither half can be removed, disabled, or quietly shrunk from inside the drill.
- **Findings 3, 4, 12: the drill's own honesty.** Broken subjects must now be refused **by name** — five specific refusal sentences rather than a generic FAIL marker, so a subject that crashes for an unrelated reason is not scored as a detection. The drill's child processes gained a 300-second timeout and a hang or signal is a throw, not a pass. And both PASS markers moved to the very end of the run, because a marker printed before the remaining work is a claim about work that has not happened.
- **Findings 9, 10, 11: three guards that read one spelling.** (9) The YAML key parser compared raw text, so `"\x69f": false` — the key `if` to any parser — disabled a governed step with the guard green; double-quoted scalars are now **decoded** before they are judged. (10) The nonce single-assignment count matched only `^ *$nonce = `, which PowerShell spells at least four other ways (`${nonce} =`, `$nonce=`, `$script:nonce =`, `Set-Variable nonce`); every write form is now counted and any extra write is red. (11) The NBSP tokenizer row named a **variable**, so pinning the row's text said nothing about the character it carried — rewriting `$nonBreakingSpace = [char]0x00A0` to an ASCII space kept the pin green and turned the one case that distinguishes Windows' separators from `[char]::IsWhiteSpace` into a case about an ordinary space. Both the code-point definition and its single assignment are now required.
- **A guard that counted call sites was itself wrong, and the gate said so.** SR-070's guard pinned nine `Assert-MapleSeasonCase` call sites; the file has **eight** — five portable, one inside the gutted-predicate failure loop, and two inside the Windows-only loops. The count is corrected to eight with its arithmetic spelled out, and `Assert-True` may still appear exactly twice: the deliberate canary self-test and the call inside the wrapper.
- **Running the gates found two stale needles, loudly, which is the designed behaviour.** Rewriting the workflow's mutation-drill step and the ownership manifest format left three of the drill's own needles stale, and `mutate()` refused each by name rather than silently testing unmodified source. One further consequence had to be repaired in the guard: the orchestrator's mutation-drill lane no longer goes through `Invoke-FoundationLane`, because that helper streams its child's output and does not return it, and the lane must now **read** what the drill printed. The lane-statement count drops 24 → 23 and the lane is pinned by the behavioural claim it holds instead.
- **Four of SR-070's claims corrected.** (a) "Two independent reporting channels cross-checked by a `throw`" — they were not independent in the way that mattered; both counted failures only, and a skipped passing assertion was invisible to both. (b) "The counter is declared before the Windows branch and consumed after it closes, so wrapping the branch body cannot take the check with it" — true of the *consumption* and false of the *measurement*: the counter incremented before the comparison, so the check survived and measured nothing. (c) F8/the ubuntu lane was recorded as an accepted residual when it was a red CI lane on that commit. (d) SR-070's own residual paragraph said the counter "still fired in the drill above, because an unset variable compares unequal to six" and called that "fail-closed by accident" — accurate as far as it went, but it described the weaker of the two problems; the counter's placement, not its declaration site, was the defect.

### Evidence — every claim above was run and watched, on this workstation, at the repaired tree

- **Sabotage re-planted against the FIXED files, each defeat required to be refused BY NAME** (`scratchpad/verify-sol071-after-fix.mjs`; a generic FAIL was not accepted, because the defect class this round repaired is a gate that goes green on a wrapped block):
  - *Defeat 6* — the tokenizer comparison wrapped in `if ($false) { … }`, the receipt statement left untouched. The receipt now reads `TOKENIZER_RECEIPT comparisons=0 distinct=0 windows=true`, the preflight regression exits **1**, the static guard exits **1**, and the refusal names it: `… command lines to CommandLineToArgvW instead of 33`. Before the repair this exact plant printed `comparisons=33` and exited `0`.
  - *Defeat 5* — a single assertion that normally **passes** (`Two refusal rows share a label`) wrapped. The ownership suite exits **1**, prints **no manifest at all** (it refuses before publishing), and the refusal names it: `… assertion cases and expected at least …`. The static guard stays green on this one by design — the plant adds no forbidden text — which is precisely why the count had to move into the suite's own runtime.
  - *Defeat 1* — `$onWindows` forced false and the predicate made to refuse every listener. The suite exits **0** with `OWNERSHIP_MANIFEST … windows=false windowsCases=0 cases=5 challenges=0 canary=caught`. **The blind spot is confirmed as real**: it cannot be closed by an assertion on this platform, so the drill declares it (`BEHAVIOUR_GAP`, `unseenByBehaviour`) instead of throwing.
  - Restored by file copy from snapshots of the current files — never `git checkout`, never `bak070`. Static guards exit **0** afterwards and `git status --porcelain` shows exactly the six files this round edits and nothing else.
- **The ubuntu lane simulated as faithfully as this workstation allows** (`scratchpad/verify-ubuntu-lane-071.mjs`; the platform is decided in two places, so both `onWindows` in the drill and `$onWindows` in the suite were forced false, and the child shell is `pwsh` 7): drill exits **0**; prints `Foundation mutation drill: PASS (147 controlled mutations turned the gate red)`; prints the gap by name — `BEHAVIOUR_GAP kill-authorizing predicate refuses everything: the must-be-TRUE table is Windows-only, so off Windows the suite stays green on a predicate that declares every listener foreign …`; and prints `Foundation behavioural mutation drill: PASS (4 broken subjects were reported by the suite that runs against them, 1 not measurable on this platform)` — **string-equal** to the `$expectedBehaviour` extracted from `.github/workflows/foundation.yml`, which is the whole point: the caller holds the sentence, the drill has to earn it.
- **Standalone runs at the repaired tree, all exit 0:** `node scripts/foundation-static-guards.mjs` → `Foundation static guards: PASS`. `scripts/maple-season-browser-port-preflight.regression.ps1` → `TOKENIZER_RECEIPT comparisons=33 distinct=33 windows=true` + PASS. `scripts/maple-season-browser-ownership.regression.ps1` → `OWNERSHIP_MANIFEST tokenizer=29 refusals=25 gutted=25 windows=true windowsCases=24 cases=29 challenges=0 canary=caught` + PASS. `node scripts/verify-foundation-mutations.mjs` → `Foundation mutation drill: PASS (147 controlled mutations turned the gate red)` and `Foundation behavioural mutation drill: PASS (5 broken subjects were reported by the suite that runs against them, 0 not measurable on this platform)`, all five behavioural subjects refused by their own named sentence.
- **The whole foundation gate, end to end: `scripts/verify-foundation.ps1` exit 0**, 1,308 log lines (`scratchpad/foundation-071.log`). Line 166 `Foundation static guards: PASS`; line 320 the 147-mutation marker; line 321 the orchestrator's 5-broken/0-gap behavioural marker; line 323 `OWNERSHIP_MANIFEST … cases=29 challenges=6 canary=caught`; lines 324–329 all six challenge answers carrying the same run nonce `6d4706d0e12349ab81393bada51dc73b`; line 1307 Playwright `62 passed (48.2s)` with 14 skipped; line 1308 `Farm Rx foundation gate: PASS`.
- **Forced TypeScript: `npx tsc -b --force` exit 0** (plain `tsc --noEmit` is not sufficient in this repository and was not relied on).
- `TOKENIZER_RECEIPT` deliberately does **not** appear in the gate log. The July caller requires its child's entire output to equal its PASS marker, so the receipt is asserted there longhand — `'TOKENIZER_RECEIPT comparisons=33 distinct=33 windows=true'` at line 100 of `scripts/maple-july-db-clock-wiring.regression.ps1` — rather than streamed. That is two independent consumers of the same receipt, which is what limits the residual below.

### Open, not closed — stated so no reader mistakes this entry for completion

- **Finding 7 is a residual.** The July caller's own receipt assertion could itself be wrapped. It is mitigated, not fixed: the receipt is consumed in two places by two files that hold the expected string longhand, so defeating it needs edits in both. Fixing it properly means the caller measuring that it performed its own comparison — the same move again, one layer up — and that is a decision about where this recursion stops, not a repair I should make unasked.
- **F8 remains a real gap in CI, now measured rather than assumed.** `.github/workflows/foundation.yml` runs on `ubuntu-latest`; the kill-authorizing predicate's must-be-TRUE table is Windows-only. The lane is green and honest about what it cannot see, but **no CI runner exercises the assertions that protect Mason's workstation from a false TRUE**. Closing it needs a Windows CI job — Mason's call, and still unanswered.
- **The predicate findings named in SR-062 through SR-070 are still open and still unfixed:** F1 (any argument anywhere inside the tree establishes ownership), F3/F4 (junctions and symlinks unresolved, `OrdinalIgnoreCase`), F6 (exact-root truncation fails open), F5/F7 (subst, 8.3 and UNC aliases plus the blanket `""` refusal produce false FALSEs), F24 (the workstation observations are not reproducible from the repository alone).
- **The cleanup path itself — the actual workstation-safety defect — is still queued** (F13–F18, drafted in `scratchpad/clear-port-draft.ps1`: `Get-MapleSeasonPortListener` replacing the fail-open `SilentlyContinue` probe, a 10-FILETIME-tick creation check, validate-all-before-terminate, and a handle-pinned `TerminateProcess`).
