Six rounds of this tranche have hardened the *proof* that the predicate is tested; they have not yet changed what the force kill does. That is the next tranche, and it is worth more than a seventh layer of guard-the-guard.
- **Decisions waiting on Mason, unchanged:** the Job Object rewrite together with F1's ownership semantics (one decision, not two); a Windows CI job; and wiring the nine orphaned PowerShell regressions into a gate behind a blanket "every regression must have a caller" guard — which would turn the gate red today, which is the point of proposing it rather than doing it.
- **Authority, stated plainly:** local commit only, on `claude/gauntlet-testing-sweep-013d65`. Nothing was pushed, merged, or deployed, and no approval is inferred from the gate being green.

## SR-072 — Sol REQUEST-CHANGES on `c87b12b` repaired: layer seven is that a *value* can be fabricated, an *expectation* can be overwritten by the party being judged, and a *cmdlet* can be shadowed; plus six of SR-071's claims corrected, including one that was a live CI break

- **Date/time:** 2026-08-06 (`America/Chicago`).
- **Authority:** local repair work only. Mason's authorization covers local commits on worktree branch `claude/gauntlet-testing-sweep-013d65`. The earlier repair-plan approval question remains unanswered, the structural decisions raised in SR-062 through SR-071 remain unanswered, and no outward action was taken or is claimed as approved. This entry corrects SR-071 by appending; SR-060 through SR-071 are left exactly as written.
- **Origin:** a fresh-context read-only Sol review of the exact commit `c87b12b` returned REQUEST-CHANGES with ten findings — three critical, four high, two medium, and one about SR-071's own honesty. Eight are closed below; one is closed only in part and one is deliberately left as a bounded limit rather than counted as done. Sol independently **confirmed** six counts SR-071 had corrected (`Invoke-FoundationLane`=23, ownership wrapper call sites=8, direct `Assert-True`=2, portable tokenizer rows=29, literal rows=26, live rows=33) and reproduced static-guard PASS with `cases=29`.
- **The defect is layer seven, and it splits three ways.** SR-071's answer to layer six — "a counter placed before the thing it measures proves nothing" — was to record values rather than ticks, and to have callers hold the expectations. Sol defeated all three halves of that: **(a)** a *value* can be fabricated at its source, so recording one proves nothing more than a tick did; **(b)** an *expectation* held in a plain variable can be overwritten by the very output it is judging; **(c)** a *cmdlet* can be redefined, so a comparison written as a pipeline filter can be made to filter nothing. And a fourth, one layer out: **a self-reporting drill can stub out its own subject runner.**
- **Finding 3, reproduced and confirmed — the shadowed cmdlet.** Both callers selected the accepted challenge answer with `@($forIndex | Where-Object { $candidates -ccontains $_ })`. Eight lines placed *earlier in the same file* — `function Where-Object { process { $_ } }` — return every object un-filtered, so a child printing `owned=FALSE` against a caller expecting `TRUE` is accepted, `$accepted.Count` is 1, and the reconciliation compares the forged answer with itself. Every pin stayed green. The repair rewrites both selections as a `foreach` keyword loop with `-ccontains` — a language keyword and an operator, neither of which a function can shadow — and adds a **prohibition** guard over nine shadowable cmdlets in all three PowerShell files, refusing `function`, `Set-Alias`/`New-Alias`, and `$function:`/`$alias:` provider writes. This is the one shape of static check that is sound alone: unlike an affirmation, the forbidden thing cannot take effect without its definition being present for the guard to find. That distinction is now written into the guard's comments so it is not re-derived a fourth time.
- **Finding 2, confirmed — the static half was decoration.** The drill's 147 static mutations lived at lines 69–632 and printed `Foundation mutation drill: PASS (…)`, and **no caller read that sentence**. The whole static half could be wrapped in `if (false)` and both callers would still be satisfied by the behavioural sentence alone. Both callers now hold the static sentence with its count and refuse a run without it — and the drill now mutates each caller's consumption of it, so removing either check is itself a detected mutation.
- **Finding 6 was a live CI break with no adversarial edit at all, and this was my error, not a residual.** SR-071 repaired the ubuntu lane by making the drill's expectation platform-conditional, then simulated only the drill **step**. The same ubuntu job runs `scripts/verify-foundation.ps1` afterwards, and that script demanded the 5-broken/0-gap sentence *unconditionally* while the step demanded 4-broken/1-gap. The lane was red on `c87b12b` on either platform's truth. The repair has the orchestrator hold **both** exact sentences, select by platform, and **refuse the other platform's sentence** — a run that prints both is satisfying its callers rather than measuring anything, because at most one can be true of the machine it ran on. And the harness was replaced: `scratchpad/verify-ubuntu-job-072.mjs` simulates the **whole job**, extracting the orchestrator's lane statements from the real file rather than retyping them, because retyping is how a harness drifts from the thing it claims to prove.
- **Finding 4, confirmed — the fabricated value.** SR-071's `TOKENIZER_RECEIPT comparisons=33 distinct=33` was defeated by writing `$agrees = $true` immediately after the `$null` clear and wrapping both parses: `$agrees` is truthy with no call to `CommandLineToArgvW`, and both counts still reach 33. The repair adds `tokens=`, which sums the argument counts Windows **returned** — an unset `$expected` counts zero, so no number of extra assignments to the agreement flag can reach the total. The expected total is **90**, and it is 90 because the run printed 90: my first guess of 111 was refused by the assertion I had just written, by name. The comment on that line says it was measured rather than reasoned about, because that is the honest provenance of the number.
- **Finding 7, confirmed — two spellings the write counter could not see.** SR-071 claimed "every write form is now counted." PowerShell variable names are **case-insensitive**, and `${script:nonce}` puts the scope qualifier *inside* the braces; Sol confirmed both `${script:nonce} = 'fixed'` and `${script:nonBreakingSpace} = [char]0x20` took effect with the count still reading one. Both spellings are now handled, but the load-bearing barrier moved out of the regex entirely: every protected value is a `Set-Variable -Option ReadOnly` binding, so **PowerShell itself refuses the second write at run time**. A regex over another language is the wrong place for a hard barrier; the count is now the second channel, not the first. One consequence had to be repaired in the counter itself — these files quote the very defeats they defend against, so the counter was reading its own documentation as extra writes. Comment-only lines are dropped first, which can only remove false positives: a line PowerShell treats as a comment cannot assign anything, and a write sharing a line with code is still counted.
- **Finding 9, confirmed — the expectation the judged party could overwrite.** The reached-case tally was a plain array, so `$script:tallied = @('padding') * $expectedCases` placed just before the count check made the count agree with no case having run; and both callers held their expected manifest in plain variables, so the child's own manifest line could be assigned back over the expectation, making each caller compare the child's answer with itself. Both tallies are now `ReadOnly` append-only `List[string]` bindings and both callers' manifest expectations are `ReadOnly`. Deliberately **not** shared through a file: each caller writes its expectation longhand, so defeating the challenge requires editing two files.
- **Finding 5, confirmed — a fourth escape spelling in the YAML key decoder.** `"\U00000069f": false` decodes to the key `if` and disables a governed workflow step. The eight-digit `\U` branch is added, ordered longest-first so it cannot be mis-parsed as `\u` plus stray hex.
- **Finding 8, confirmed — the drill's two scoring helpers accepted too much.** `detectedByBehaviour` accepted **any** non-zero exit plus an unanchored substring, so a crash, a syntax error introduced by the mutation, or an unrelated failure all scored as detections — and would keep scoring after the mutation stopped being applied. It now requires four things: exit **exactly 1** (this suite's own FAIL code, not 2, not 255, not `null`), the sentence naming *this* defect, the **absence** of the PASS marker, and that the sentence is **new relative to the green baseline** — a sentence the suite says on unmutated source is boilerplate, not evidence. `unseenByBehaviour` accepted any exit-0 child, which is also what a suite that stopped early produces; it now requires exit 0 **plus** the PASS marker **plus** the manifest fields that make the run the off-Windows shape the gap is claimed about (`windows=false windowsCases=0 cases=5`).
- **Finding 1 is closed in part, and the part that is not closed is named rather than papered over.** Sol's critical finding: stubbing the drill's own runner — `const runOwnershipSuite = () => ({ status: 1, output: expected })` — makes all five behavioural subjects "detected" without a child process ever starting, and **the static guard did not read the drill file at all**. It does now: the guard reads `scripts/verify-foundation-mutations.mjs` and pins the child process, the returned result, the hang refusal, the green-baseline requirement, all five scoring criteria, and both PASS sentences as counted values rather than literals — and the drill mutates its own copy against each of those pins, six new cases. What this does **not** do is make a self-reporting file honest about itself. That is not reachable by a check living in the same repository the file lives in, and an eighth counter inside the drill would not reach it either. What closes it is review of the exact commit plus a protected branch running the gate on a machine the author does not control. Stated as a limit, not claimed as a fix.
- **Adding cases moved a pinned number, and the gate said so.** The static drill went 147 → **172** mutations, which is held as a literal in three places; two comments still carrying 147 were corrected to say "every one of its mutations" rather than a number that goes stale on the next case. One pin had to be **split**: the caller's expected-manifest pin was checking two independent properties — the `ReadOnly` binding and the `canary=caught` requirement — under a single label, so the drill could not say which had broken. They are now two labels.
- **A needle that matched itself, measured and named.** The six new drill cases over the drill's own file first used plain substrings, and each one matched **its own needle text**: the needle is written out in the file above the code it aims at, so `String.replace` mutated the mutation case and left the runner untouched. The guard was green, the drill reported "not detected", and the symptom read like a blind guard rather than a self-hit needle. All six are now anchored to line start, which works because the copy inside a needle is preceded by a quote and never by line-start-plus-spaces. That reasoning is in the file, because this is the second distinct way a stale-or-wrong needle has presented in this tranche.
- **Six of SR-071's claims corrected.** (a) "neither half can be removed, disabled, or quietly shrunk from inside the drill" — false for the static half, which no caller read (finding 2). (b) "every write form is now counted and any extra write is red" — false for case-differing names and braced-scope writes, both confirmed to land silently (finding 7). (c) "Broken subjects must now be refused **by name** … so a subject that crashes for an unrelated reason is not scored as a detection" — false: any non-zero exit plus an unanchored substring scored, so a crash did (finding 8). (d) "records the comparison only inside `if ($agrees) { … }` — a value that exists only because the comparison ran" — false: `$agrees` could be forced true at its source (finding 4). (e) "both callers now select the accepted answer by filtering their **own** candidate spellings, record `$accepted[0]` — a value the comparison produced" — false while the filter was a shadowable cmdlet (finding 3). (f) "**The ubuntu lane simulated as faithfully as this workstation allows**" — the simulation covered the drill *step* only; the same job's orchestrator lane demanded the other platform's sentence, so the lane was red (finding 6). SR-071 stands as written; this paragraph is the correction.

### Evidence — every claim above was run and watched, on this workstation, at the repaired tree

- **`node scripts/foundation-static-guards.mjs` → `Foundation static guards: PASS`, exit 0**, after the guard was made to read the drill file and gained the cmdlet-shadowing prohibition, the split canary pins, and the receipt/tally/nonce `ReadOnly` pins.
- **`node scripts/verify-foundation-mutations.mjs` → exit 0**: `Foundation mutation drill: PASS (172 controlled mutations turned the gate red)` and `Foundation behavioural mutation drill: PASS (5 broken subjects were reported by the suite that runs against them, 0 not measurable on this platform)`. The twenty-five new cases each turned the guard red by their own label, including all six over the drill's own file — the stubbed runner, the relaxed exit check, the accepted PASS marker, the dropped baseline-novelty check, the skipped manifest check, and the disabled green baseline.
- **The whole ubuntu job simulated, drill step *and* orchestrator lane** (`scratchpad/verify-ubuntu-job-072.mjs`; both platform branches forced — `onWindows` in the drill and `$onWindows` in the suite, which reads `$IsWindows` and cannot be forced from outside on Windows — child shell `pwsh` 7, and the orchestrator's lane statements **extracted from `scripts/verify-foundation.ps1` by anchor** with only the platform probe replaced): drill exit **0**; static sentence string-equal to the workflow's `$expectedStatic`; `BEHAVIOUR_GAP kill-authorizing predicate refuses everything: …` printed by name; behavioural sentence string-equal to the workflow's `$expectedBehaviour`; the refused sentence **absent**; and the orchestrator lane exit **0**, reaching `ORCHESTRATOR_LANE_ACCEPTED_THE_UBUNTU_DRILL_OUTPUT`. Verdict `GREEN`.
- **The same harness proved to have teeth, by re-planting finding 6.** With the orchestrator's platform selection replaced by `$expectedBehaviouralMarker = $windowsBehaviouralMarker`, the static guard exits **1** naming `orchestrator:mutation-drill-claim-selected-by-platform`, and the harness exits **1** with the orchestrator lane refusing the ubuntu drill output. Finding 6 can no longer be reintroduced without the guard going red — which is a stronger result than the harness alone, and it is why the harness's negative control also names the guard. Restored by file copy from a snapshot taken in that run; static guards exit **0** afterwards.
- **Standalone regression runs at the repaired tree, both exit 0:** `scripts/maple-season-browser-port-preflight.regression.ps1` → `TOKENIZER_RECEIPT comparisons=33 distinct=33 tokens=90 windows=true` + PASS. `scripts/maple-season-browser-ownership.regression.ps1` → `OWNERSHIP_MANIFEST tokenizer=29 refusals=25 gutted=25 windows=true windowsCases=24 cases=29 challenges=0 canary=caught` + PASS.
- **Forced TypeScript: `npx tsc -b --force` exit 0** (plain `tsc --noEmit` is not sufficient in this repository and was not relied on).
- **The whole foundation gate, end to end: `scripts/verify-foundation.ps1` exit 0, `Farm Rx foundation gate: PASS`**, 1333 log lines. Inside that run, by line: `Foundation static guards: PASS`; `Foundation mutation drill: PASS (172 controlled mutations turned the gate red)`; `Foundation behavioural mutation drill: PASS (5 broken subjects were reported by the suite that runs against them, 0 not measurable on this platform)` — the Windows sentence, correctly selected, with the ubuntu sentence absent; `Foundation Windows lane runtime drill: PASS (6 of 6 cases executed and validated on win32)`; `OWNERSHIP_MANIFEST tokenizer=29 refusals=25 gutted=25 windows=true windowsCases=24 cases=29 challenges=6 canary=caught` followed by all six challenge answers and `MAPLE_SEASON_BROWSER_OWNERSHIP_REGRESSION_PASS`; and Playwright `62 passed, 14 skipped` across `chromium-phone` and `chromium-desktop`.
- **The gate answered the one question that matters about Mason's workstation, by name.** `OWNERSHIP_CHALLENGE 1 owned=FALSE argv=<C:\Program Files\nodejs\node.exe>|<scripts/factory-board.mjs>|<--port>|<4177>` — the live unrelated process on this workstation, on the very port the cleanup path targets, refused. Challenges 4 and 5 both carry the same run nonce `79278b3a2be64201888ecee239ffb9ce`, answered TRUE and FALSE respectively, so the reconciliation compared a value produced during this run rather than a constant: challenge 2's quote-inside-path case (`<C:\FarmRx">|<Backup\x.js>`) and challenge 3's `C:\FarmRx2` prefix case were both refused.
- **One honest gap in that evidence, named rather than glossed.** `scripts/maple-season-browser-port-preflight.regression.ps1` has **no caller in `scripts/verify-foundation.ps1` or in the workflow** — it is one of the nine orphaned regressions. Its *contents* are pinned by the static guard and its rules are drilled by 25-plus mutation cases; its *tokenizer behaviour* is exercised inside the gate indirectly, through the ownership regression's 29 tokenizer cases. But its own 33-row live receipt (`tokens=90`) runs only when invoked by hand, as it was above. So the receipt is real and the number is measured, and it is **not** something a gate run would catch going missing. That is the orphaned-regression decision waiting on Mason, restated with the specific file it costs.

### Open, not closed — stated so no reader mistakes this entry for completion

- **Finding 1's unbounded half is a limit, not a repair.** Any self-reporting file can be edited to lie about itself, and no check inside the same repository reaches that. The guard now pins the drill's runner and every scoring criterion, so the accident and the quiet edit are covered; the deliberate author is not. The answer is review of the exact commit plus a protected branch running this on a machine the author does not control — which is a decision about CI, not a test I can write.
- **This is the seventh consecutive round of hardening the *proof* rather than the *defect*.** Seven layers have now been closed — substring pins, the marker-is-a-string, the transport, the assertion helper, existence-versus-execution, counter placement, and now fabricated values / overwritable expectations / shadowable cmdlets. **None of them has changed what the force kill does.** The recommendation stands and is repeated here so it is on the record: stop the guard-the-guard recursion and take up the cleanup-path tranche next.
- **The cleanup path is still queued** (F13–F18, drafted in `scratchpad/clear-port-draft.ps1`: `Get-MapleSeasonPortListener` replacing the fail-open `SilentlyContinue` probe, a 10-FILETIME-tick creation check, validate-all-before-terminate, and a handle-pinned `TerminateProcess`, plus the deterministic two-listener behavioural test — enumeration is IPv6-first, so that test must read the order at run time rather than assume it). This is the defect that can damage Mason's workstation.
- **F8 remains a real gap in CI.** `.github/workflows/foundation.yml` runs on `ubuntu-latest`; the predicate's must-be-TRUE table is Windows-only. The lane is now green *and* honest about what it cannot see, and the blind spot is asserted rather than skipped — but **no CI runner exercises the assertions that protect Mason's workstation from a false TRUE**. Closing it needs a Windows CI job. Mason's call, still unanswered.
- **Finding 7's residual from SR-071 is unchanged.** The July caller's own receipt assertion could still be wrapped. Mitigated, not fixed: the receipt is consumed by two files that each hold the expected string longhand, so defeating it needs edits in both.
- **The predicate findings named in SR-062 through SR-071 are still open and still unfixed:** F1 (any argument anywhere inside the tree establishes ownership), F3/F4 (junctions and symlinks unresolved, `OrdinalIgnoreCase`), F6 (exact-root truncation fails open), F5/F7 (subst, 8.3 and UNC aliases plus the blanket `""` refusal produce false FALSEs), F24 (the workstation observations are not reproducible from the repository alone).
- **Decisions waiting on Mason, unchanged:** the Job Object rewrite together with F1's ownership semantics (one decision, not two); a Windows CI job; and wiring the nine orphaned PowerShell regressions into a gate behind a blanket "every regression must have a caller" guard — which would turn the gate red today, which is the point of proposing it rather than doing it.
- **What Sol said it could not determine, recorded so this entry does not borrow authority it lacks:** read-only, Sol could not establish the real Windows socket/preflight outcome, the full mutation or foundation run, GitHub's actual ubuntu execution, or authenticate any historical "ran and watched" claim. Every runtime claim in this entry rests on this workstation's own runs, named above.
- **Authority, stated plainly:** local commit only, on `claude/gauntlet-testing-sweep-013d65`. Nothing was pushed, merged, or deployed, and no approval is inferred from the gate being green.

## SR-073 — the cleanup path itself repaired (F7, F13–F15, F17, F18): a fail-closed listener probe, a handle-pinned termination, and validate-all-before-terminate; plus Sol finding 1 proven in both directions and seven of SR-072's claims corrected

- **Date/time:** 2026-08-06 (`America/Chicago`).
- **Authority:** local repair work and one local commit on worktree branch `claude/gauntlet-testing-sweep-013d65`. Nothing was pushed, merged, or deployed, and no approval is inferred from the gate being green. The earlier repair-plan approval question remains unanswered, and the structural decisions raised in SR-062 through SR-072 remain unanswered. This entry corrects SR-072 by appending; SR-060 through SR-072 are left exactly as written.
- **Origin:** SR-072's own recommendation — "stop the guard-the-guard recursion and take up the cleanup-path tranche next" — plus the fresh-context read-only Sol REQUEST-CHANGES on exact commit `71742a8`, whose finding 1 is closed here with a two-run negative control and whose finding 9 is closed by disproving it.
- **This is the first tranche in eight that changes what the force kill DOES, not what proves it.** Seven prior rounds hardened the proof. `Clear-MapleSeasonBrowserPort` is now a different piece of code:
  - **F7 — the probe fails CLOSED.** Both callers used to query the port with `-ErrorAction SilentlyContinue` and read the resulting empty list as "nothing is listening", so a query that *failed* reported the port clean and the cleanup reported done. All queries now go through `Get-MapleSeasonPortListener` with `-ErrorAction Stop`. **Measured:** a genuinely free port raises `Microsoft.PowerShell.Cmdletization.Cim.CimJobException` with `FullyQualifiedErrorId` `CmdletizationQuery_NotFound,Get-NetTCPConnection`, so "free" *is* distinguishable from "the query broke". Only that one error id means empty; every other error throws by name.
  - **F18 — there is exactly ONE `Get-NetTCPConnection` left in the file.** A static guard counts the call sites and fails if a second appears, so no caller can reintroduce a fail-open probe of its own. That count is drilled: adding a second direct query turns the guard red by the exact rendered id `season-browser:listener-probe-is-the-only-net-query (found 2 Get-NetTCPConnection call sites, expected 1 - the one inside Get-MapleSeasonPortListener)`.
  - **F13 — the kill goes through a handle opened BEFORE the ownership check.** **Measured:** a .NET `Process` object pins nothing — `haveProcessHandle` stayed `False` and `m_processHandle` stayed `null` across `.StartTime`, `.HasExited` and `.Kill()`, so every one of those calls re-resolved the process id and the id could have been recycled between the check and the kill. What reserves a process id on Windows is an **open OS handle**. So the cleanup calls `OpenProcess` first, validates ownership against *that* handle's process, and terminates with `TerminateProcess($target.Handle, 1)` — never a second look-up.
  - **F14 — a 10-FILETIME-tick creation check** confirms the handle's process is the one that was validated. **Measured across 304 live processes on this workstation:** the maximum CIM-vs-kernel creation-time disagreement was 9 ticks, median 4.
  - **F15 — every listener is validated before ANY is terminated.** **Measured:** one port can hold two listeners (one on `127.0.0.1`, one on `::1`), and `Get-NetTCPConnection` returns them **IPv6-first, not in creation order**. Terminating each as it validated meant a mixed port — one Farm Rx listener, one foreign — could lose the Farm Rx listener and *then* have the run refuse and report that it terminated nothing.
- **F15 proven by negative control, not by a green test.** Reintroducing F15 on a copy-backed edit of the tracked file (terminate as soon as each listener validates) made the regression exit **1** with its own diagnosis: `Mixed-listener cleanup terminated its OWN listener on port 4289 and then reported that it refused to terminate anything; a refusal must validate every listener before terminating any.` Restored from the in-run snapshot: exit **0**, `TOKENIZER_RECEIPT comparisons=33 distinct=33 tokens=90 windows=true` plus PASS. Verdict `GREEN`.
- **Sol finding 1, confirmed, measured, and proven in BOTH directions.** SR-072's `tokens=` receipt was supposed to make a fabricated agreement flag useless, and its comment claimed "an unset `$expected` counts zero". That was **false when written**: only `$agrees` was cleared per row, so skipping one row's two parses left `$expected` holding the **previous** row's argument array. Whether that is detectable depends on whether the neighbouring rows parse to the same number of arguments, so it was measured rather than argued: **33 rows, 90 tokens, and 13 adjacent pairs with equal argument counts**, including the tab-separated row Sol named. The carry therefore reconciles to exactly `tokens=90` and the receipt reads clean. Per-row counts: `[0]=2 [1]=4 [2]=4 [3]=4 [4]=2 [5]=4 [6]=2 [7]=2 [8]=2 [9]=3 [10]=3 [11]=3 [12]=2 [13]=3 [14]=2 [15]=6 [16]=5 [17]=3 [18]=2 [19]=2 [20]=2 [21]=2 [22]=2 [23]=2 [24]=1 [25]=2 [26]=2 [27]=4 [28]=3 [29]=2 [30]=4 [31]=2 [32]=2`. **Run A** (pre-fix shape, one row's parses skipped) printed the full `TOKENIZER_RECEIPT comparisons=33 distinct=33 tokens=90 windows=true` and `MAPLE_SEASON_BROWSER_PORT_PREFLIGHT_REGRESSION_PASS` at exit **0** — the defect live and completely unreported. **Run B** (fixed, same row skipped) exited **1** naming the exact row: "The tokenizer comparison recorded a receipt for 'node.exe→C:\FarmRx\x.js→--port→4177' without a parse from CommandLineToArgvW in this iteration…". Restore: exit **0**. The repair is two parts, and both were needed: all three of `$agrees`/`$expected`/`$actual` cleared per row, and an **affirmative** assertion inside the recording gate that this iteration obtained a parse at all. Clearing alone only under-counts; the assertion is what makes the shortfall *say so* at the row rather than two hundred lines later as a total that does not reconcile.
- **A host-dependent assertion that had already failed a whole gate run, fixed by asserting content instead of a count.** `CommandLineToArgvW('')` answers with the path of the process that **asked**, tokenized on spaces. **Measured:** under Windows PowerShell 5.1 the host is `C:\Windows\System32\WindowsPowerShell\v1.0\powershell.exe` and the answer is **1** argument; under PowerShell 7 it is `C:\Program Files\PowerShell\7\pwsh.exe` and the answer is **2** (`C:\Program` and `Files\PowerShell\7\pwsh.exe`). `Get-FoundationProbeShell` hands this chain `pwsh.exe` whenever the orchestrator itself runs on PowerShell 7, so a pin on the count failed the gate with a message about Windows changing behaviour when nothing had changed but which shell started it — a false FALSE with a wrong diagnosis, the exact failure mode this suite exists to prevent. The assertion now rejoins the arguments and compares them to the asking process's own module path, which reconstructs on both hosts and still proves the point: the answer is built from the caller and owes nothing to the input.
- **Four stale needles, and all four failed in the SAFE direction.** Rewriting the cleanup path broke four text needles that described the old code: `verify-foundation-mutations.mjs`'s `$ownedProcess.Kill()` mutation, `maple-july-db-clock-wiring.regression.ps1:77`'s `$ownedProcess.Kill()` assertion, and **both** loopback drills in `verify-season-contract.regression.mjs`, which anchored on two separate `Get-NetTCPConnection` call sites that no longer exist. Every one of them went **red and named itself** rather than silently passing over source it no longer describes — which is why `mutate()` throws `Mutation no longer applies to …; its needle is stale` on a no-op, and why the July caller is worth keeping alongside the static guard: it runs inside the gate's Windows lane, so a stale needle there fails a **gate run**, not only a hand-run check. This is now the fourth distinct presentation of the stale-needle failure mode in this tranche and the reasoning is written into each file.
- **The two season-contract loopback drills collapse into one, honestly.** They existed as a pair because two functions each held a query and `String.replace` rewrites only the first match. There is now one shared probe, so there is one place left where the address filter can be narrowed, and one drill covers it: `Season isolation mutation governed-port listener probe narrowed to loopback: EXPECTED FAIL (scripts/maple-season-browser.ps1 port checks do not fail closed for wildcard or IPv6 listeners.)`. First-match-only is not a hazard for this drill the way it was for the pair, because the contract rule it exercises is a **whole-file prohibition** on `Get-NetTCPConnection -LocalAddress` — narrowing any occurrence anywhere trips it. The pair's real property, that each function's own query got mutated, is now true by construction. Isolation mutations therefore go 18 → **17**, which is a reduction in drill count and not in coverage, and it is recorded here so the lower number is not read later as a regression.
- **Sol finding 9 verified, and SR-072's own conclusion about it disproved.** SR-072:922 stated that `scripts/maple-season-browser-port-preflight.regression.ps1` "has **no caller** in `scripts/verify-foundation.ps1` or in the workflow" and that its 33-row live receipt "runs only when invoked by hand". That is **wrong**. The chain is `scripts/verify-foundation.ps1:86` → `scripts/maple-july-db-clock-wiring.regression.ps1:101` → the preflight regression, and line 111 of that caller asserts the receipt **exactly**, with the expected two lines held longhand at 107–110 including `TOKENIZER_RECEIPT comparisons=33 distinct=33 tokens=90 windows=true`. The reason SR-072 got it wrong is discoverable rather than mysterious: line 101 **captures** the child's output into a variable and never prints it, so `TOKENIZER_RECEIPT` does not appear in a gate log to be grepped. `MAPLE_JULY_DB_CLOCK_WIRING_REGRESSION_PASS` at line 398 of this tranche's gate log is the proof that the chain executed. The narrower cost that *is* real: CI runs `ubuntu-latest`, this lane is Windows-only, so no CI runner ever binds the sockets or calls the live `CommandLineToArgvW` table.
- **Seven of SR-072's claims corrected.** (a) SR-072:922's orphaned-preflight claim, disproved above — the file has a caller inside the gate; the receipt is captured, not printed. (b) SR-072:903, "**PowerShell itself refuses the second write at run time**" — false for `ReadOnly`. **Measured:** `ReadOnly` plus `Set-Variable -Force` **overrides**; `ReadOnly` plus `Remove-Variable -Force` then recreate **succeeds**; only `Constant` refuses both. (c) SR-072:904, "**`ReadOnly` append-only `List[string]`**" — a `Constant`-bound `List[string]` is still mutable: `Clear()` followed by `AddRange` took the count to 2. `Constant` protects the **reference**, not the contents, so the append-only property rests on the guard and the drill, not on the binding. (d) SR-072:899, the prohibition being "**the one shape of static check that is sound alone**" — false. **Measured:** both `function global:Where-Object` and `filter Where-Object` defeat the filter (count 3, not 0) and **both evade the current regex**. (e) SR-072:902, "**an unset `$expected` counts zero**" — false when written, per finding 1 above. (f) SR-072:907, "the drill mutates its own copy against each of those pins, **six new cases**" — the guard holds **13** `mutation-drill:*` pins and the drill drills **6**, so **7 are undrilled**, named individually below. (g) SR-072:929's premise names the predicate's "must-be-TRUE table" as what CI cannot see, but the false-**TRUE** protection is a different table. **Measured:** `$ownedCases` = **14** rows (must-be-TRUE, catching false FALSEs), `$resolverRefusals` = **10** rows (must-be-FALSE, catching false TRUEs, Windows-only), `$portableRefusals` = **25** rows (run on every platform). The **conclusion** stands — no CI runner exercises the 10 Windows-only refusals that protect Mason's workstation from a false TRUE — but 25 portable refusals *do* run off-Windows, so CI is not entirely blind to false TRUEs. Sol's count here was also off, and it was checked rather than repeated.

### Evidence — every claim above was run and watched, on this workstation, at the repaired tree

- **The whole foundation gate, end to end: `npm run verify:foundation` printed `Farm Rx foundation gate: PASS`**, 1342 log lines, exit 0. Inside that run, by line: `found 0 vulnerabilities` (169); `Foundation static guards: PASS` (170); `Foundation mutation drill: PASS (178 controlled mutations turned the gate red)` (355); `Foundation behavioural mutation drill: PASS (5 broken subjects were reported by the suite that runs against them, 0 not measurable on this platform)` (356) — the Windows sentence, correctly selected, with the ubuntu sentence absent; `Foundation Windows lane runtime drill: PASS (6 of 6 cases executed and validated on win32)` (357); `OWNERSHIP_MANIFEST tokenizer=29 refusals=25 gutted=25 windows=true windowsCases=24 cases=29 challenges=6 canary=caught` (358) with all six challenge answers and `MAPLE_SEASON_BROWSER_OWNERSHIP_REGRESSION_PASS` (365); `Season fixture contract: PASS (101 fixtures; 6 scenarios; 6 isolation-scanned files)` (366); `Season contract regressions: PASS (9 rejected contract mutations; 17 rejected isolation mutations)` (396); `MAPLE_JULY_DB_CLOCK_WIRING_REGRESSION_PASS` (398); and Playwright `62 passed, 14 skipped` across `chromium-phone` and `chromium-desktop`. Forced TypeScript (`npx tsc -b --force`) runs inside this gate at line 235 and passed.

Challenges 4 and 5 both carry the same run nonce `6207b2c9169c4239a4f00d8233742889`, answered TRUE and FALSE, so the reconciliation compared a value produced during this run rather than a constant.

The pinned static count is held as a literal in three places (`scripts/verify-foundation.ps1:260`, `scripts/foundation-static-guards.mjs:767`, `.github/workflows/foundation.yml:51`) and all three were moved together.

That test does a real cross-origin navigation from `http://recovery.localhost:4173`, and `playwright.config.ts` sets `retries: 0` off CI, so a single transient connection failure goes straight to red. It was **not** accepted as a flake on a single-test pass: the whole browser lane was re-run under the same parallel conditions with the same command the gate uses (`npm run test:e2e`, giving **62 passed, 14 skipped, exit 0**), and then the **entire gate** was re-run end to end to green rather than stitching two runs together. This tranche touches no application code — only PowerShell scripts, the guards, the drills, the orchestrator, and the workflow — so it could not have caused it. Recorded because it is the first browser flake this ledger has seen, and if it recurs this entry is where the first sighting is.

Standalone run of `scripts/maple-july-db-clock-wiring.regression.ps1` after its stale needle was repointed: exit 0, `MAPLE_JULY_DB_CLOCK_WIRING_REGRESSION_PASS`.
- **A PowerShell parse check that proved nothing, caught and redone.** `[ref]$e` on an undeclared variable errors **non-terminatingly** and leaves the ref null, so the check printed "PS PARSE OK" while proving nothing. Redone with `$errs = $null; $toks = $null`: PARSE OK with token counts for all three PowerShell files. Recorded because it is the same shape as the defects this tranche exists to close — a check that cannot fail.

The 10 Windows-only `$resolverRefusals` — the rows that catch a false TRUE, which is the direction that force-kills a foreign process on Mason's workstation — never execute on any CI runner. The 25 portable refusals do. Closing this needs a Windows CI job. Mason's call, still unanswered.

## SR-074 — Sol REQUEST-CHANGES on `7887fd3`, tranche A: the kill path's identity guarantees, and a presence pin that a repair defeated

Sol reviewed `7887fd3` fresh-context and read-only, returned **REQUEST-CHANGES with 14 findings**, and confirmed the core of the cleanup rewrite: inside `Clear-MapleSeasonBrowserPort` no handle is terminated before ownership validation, termination uses the exact handle that was validated, handle ownership transfers safely into `$validated`, throwing paths reach `finally`, no normal double-close exists, more than two initial listeners work, an exited listener after `OpenProcess` is skipped safely, and access-denied for another user or SYSTEM refuses before termination. Sol also reconciled every numeric constant in SR-073 and confirmed the preflight regression's caller chain is real. This tranche closes findings **1, 10, 11, 12, 13** and the two comment inaccuracies inside finding 14. Findings **3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9** and the ledger half of **14** are carried; finding **2** is Mason's decision.

- **Finding 1 refuted on mechanism, then repaired anyway — because the invariant was invisible.** Sol read the timeout path's `taskkill /PID` and the regressions' `Stop-Process -Id` as kills that could reach a **recycled** process id, since no native handle was retained. **Measured on this workstation**, by reflecting on `System.Diagnostics.Process`'s private fields: `Process.Start()` leaves `haveProcessHandle=True` and `m_processHandle` an **open** `SafeProcessHandle`, **still open after the process exits**; `Start-Process -PassThru` is identical; only `Get-Process -Id` comes back with `haveProcessHandle=False` and `m_processHandle` null. An open handle makes the kernel keep the process object and therefore keep **reserving its id**, so those kills could not have reached a recycled id. Sol's reading was wrong about the mechanism — and right that the guarantee was not stated anywhere: it was a private implementation detail of another library, and a later `$process.Dispose()` or a re-resolve through `Get-Process` would have deleted it with nothing in this repository noticing. `Invoke-MapleSeasonBrowserProof` now opens its own handle immediately after `Start()`, holds it across the wait and the kill, and closes it in a `finally` that reports a failed close as a warning without replacing the scenario's own verdict. `PROCESS_QUERY_LIMITED_INFORMATION -bor SYNCHRONIZE` only — **no `PROCESS_TERMINATE`**, so the pin cannot quietly become a second kill path.
- **A repair defeated a guard pin, and the drill caught it — this is the finding of the tranche.** The pin over the cleanup's handle was `requireText('[MapleSeasonProcessInterop]::OpenProcess(')`. Adding the second, entirely legitimate call site above **silently blinded it**: the drill renamed the cleanup call, the launch call still contained the pinned substring, the static guard stayed green, and the drill failed with `cleanup validates a listener without holding a handle that reserves its id mutation was not detected. Observed:` — an **empty** observed list. A needle that names two call sites names neither of them, and this arrived from a *repair*, not from an attacker. Both pins are now **positional**, comparing indices rather than asserting presence: the cleanup handle must be opened before `Test-MapleSeasonBrowserPortOwned`, the launch handle before `taskkill.exe /PID`. The two sites are told apart by the variable each assigns (`$handle` versus `$launchedHandle`).
- **Finding 10 closed by making the drill recreate the defect it names.** The F15 drill renamed both `foreach ($target in $validated)` headers to `$validatedSoFar` while their bodies still said `$target`. Sol was right: that breaks variable binding and proves only that a guard notices a renamed loop header — it does not move termination into the validation pass. The pin was itself a presence check, so both halves were wrong in the same way. The pin now holds the **order** of three positions — validation loop opens, then a loop over the finished `$validated` list opens, then the first `TerminateProcess` — and the drill inserts a real terminate call inside the validation pass, leaving the two-pass structure standing so nothing else can notice. That is the F15 defect exactly: kill the owned listener, then refuse on the foreign one sharing the port, having already killed.
- **Finding 11 closed by measurement on both hosts.** The free-port probe compared `-like 'CmdletizationQuery_NotFound*'`, which accepts any future not-found id from any cmdlet as "this port is free". Exact is the fail-closed direction only if the id is genuinely identical everywhere this runs, so it was measured rather than assumed: **Windows PowerShell 5.1.26100.8875 and pwsh 7.6.3 each answered a free port with the byte-identical 48-character `CmdletizationQuery_NotFound,Get-NetTCPConnection`** (`CimJobException`, category `ObjectNotFound`), and each answered an **invalid** port with `ParameterArgumentTransformationError,Get-NetTCPConnection`. The compare is now `-ceq` against the whole id, and a second drill re-widens it back to the prefix form to prove the exactness is load-bearing.
- **Finding 12 closed by tightening to the measured maximum and rewording the claim.** The creation-time bound was `-gt 10`, a round number one above the largest disagreement anything here ever observed (max **9** ticks across 304 processes, median 4, only 34 of 304 exactly equal). It is now `-gt 9`, which fails in the safe direction, and the comment no longer calls this proof of identity: it is a **stale-snapshot check** — it confirms the WMI row the predicate is about to judge was taken of the same process this handle holds. Sol was right that this comparison alone cannot prove identity and that a provider row carrying the current creation timestamp with stale `Name`/`CommandLine` fields would still pass. What closes *that* is handle-bound launch provenance, which this file does not have. **F1, still open, now named at the line.**
- **Finding 13 closed without letting the footnote overwrite the diagnosis.** Both `CloseHandle` calls in the `finally` were cast to `[void]`, so a failed close — which leaves a process id reserved for the life of the session — was invisible. Failures are now collected and reported, and the decision is one line: on a run that otherwise succeeded the leak **throws**; on a run already carrying a real failure it is a **warning**, because a footnote about cleanup of the cleanup must never replace the reason the cleanup failed. A `catch` that does nothing but record `$primaryFailure = $true` and `throw` (a bare `throw` inside `catch` rethrows the `ErrorRecord` unchanged) is what makes that distinction available inside the `finally`.
- **The fifth stale needle, again failing in the safe direction.** The finding-11 edit left the F17 drill pointing at the `-like` text that no longer exists, and `mutate()` refused it by name: `Mutation no longer applies to scripts/maple-season-browser.ps1; its needle is stale: (source) => source.replace("if ($_.FullyQualifiedErrorId -like 'CmdletizationQuery_NotFound*') { return @() }", 'return @()')`. Fifth distinct presentation of this failure mode in this sweep; fifth time it went red and named itself rather than testing unmodified source.
- **A coverage hole this tranche's own repair exposed, and closed: the launch path's SUCCESS branch was executed by nothing.** `scripts/maple-season-browser-timeout.regression.ps1` is the only suite in this repository that **executes** `Invoke-MapleSeasonBrowserProof` rather than reading it as text, and it drove exactly one of that function's two branches — the timeout. The branch **every one of the twelve Maple scenarios actually takes** (child exits zero, the governed port is cleaned up, the function returns) was carried by inspection alone, and finding 1's repair had just wrapped that whole branch in a new `try/finally` holding an OS handle. That is precisely the shape of change that breaks a success path while every static pin stays green. A second case now drives it: a child that binds the governed port, releases it, and exits 0 must leave the function returning quietly, with the child provably having run, the port free, and **no handle-leak warning** — silence being the assertion, since a leaked launch handle is only ever *reported*. Five separate assertions, five separate pins, five separate drills, because a suite that keeps four of them still prints its PASS marker. This lands inside the gate automatically: `scripts/maple-july-db-clock-wiring.regression.ps1:93` runs the file and the gate runs that.
- **The new success case proven by negative control, not by its own green.** Sabotaged on a copy-backed edit of the tracked file (`if (-not $process.HasExited -or $null -eq $process.ExitCode)` → `if ($true)`, making a clean exit throw), the regression went to `MAPLE_SEASON_BROWSER_TIMEOUT_REGRESSION_FAIL Browser launch regression failed a scenario whose child exited zero: Maple launch success regression browser process ended without a readable native exit code.` Restored from the snapshot with a file copy — never `git checkout`, which destroyed four fixes earlier in this sweep — and verified byte-wise (`sabotage present = false | real check present = true`), then re-run to `MAPLE_SEASON_BROWSER_TIMEOUT_REGRESSION_PASS`.
- **Two of finding 14's comment inaccuracies repaired in place.** `scripts/maple-season-browser.ps1` still claimed the empty-command-line result is "one argument naming powershell.exe itself" — true only of the host it was written on; the comment now records both measured hosts (5.1 → 1 argument, pwsh 7 → 2, split at the space in "Program Files") and states that the regression asserts rejoined **content** against the asking process's module path rather than a count. `scripts/foundation-static-guards.mjs` claimed Windows' two argument grammars "disagree on exactly one construct"; nothing in this repository establishes that, so it now says the construct below is the one whose disagreement was **measured** here.

### Evidence — run and watched, on this workstation, at the repaired tree

- **The whole foundation gate, end to end, twice.** Final run: `Farm Rx foundation gate: PASS`, exit 0, 1348 log lines. Inside it: `Foundation static guards: PASS` (166); `Foundation mutation drill: PASS (188 controlled mutations turned the gate red)` (361); `Foundation behavioural mutation drill: PASS (5 broken subjects were reported by the suite that runs against them, 0 not measurable on this platform)` (362); `OWNERSHIP_MANIFEST tokenizer=29 refusals=25 gutted=25 windows=true windowsCases=24 cases=29 challenges=6 canary=caught` (364) with `MAPLE_SEASON_BROWSER_OWNERSHIP_REGRESSION_PASS` (371); `Season fixture contract: PASS (101 fixtures; 6 scenarios; 6 isolation-scanned files)` (372); `Season contract regressions: PASS (9 rejected contract mutations; 17 rejected isolation mutations)` (402); `MAPLE_JULY_DB_CLOCK_WIRING_REGRESSION_PASS` (404); `Foundation Windows execution lane outcome: executed (on Windows: True)` (405); Playwright `62 passed, 14 skipped`.
- **Forced TypeScript standalone:** `npx tsc -b --force`, exit 0.
- **The static drill went 178 → 188.** Ten cases: five over the launch success branch, plus the launch-side id reservation, the launch's refusal to proceed on an unpinnable id, the re-widened creation-time window, the downgraded handle-leak report, and the re-widened not-found id compare. Two existing drills were repointed rather than added (the cleanup `OpenProcess` needle, now naming the assignment; and the F15 drill, now inserting a real terminate). The count literal is held in three places — `scripts/verify-foundation.ps1`, `scripts/foundation-static-guards.mjs`, `.github/workflows/foundation.yml` — and all three moved together, twice, as the count went 178 → 182 → 183 → 188.
- **The gate again answered the one question that matters about this workstation.** `challenges=6`, `canary=caught`, and the live unrelated `node.exe … scripts/factory-board.mjs --port 4177` process on this machine was refused by the predicate rather than claimed.
- **Standalone runs:** `scripts/maple-season-browser-port-preflight.regression.ps1` → exit 0, `TOKENIZER_RECEIPT comparisons=33 distinct=33 tokens=90 windows=true`, `MAPLE_SEASON_BROWSER_PORT_PREFLIGHT_REGRESSION_PASS`. `scripts/maple-season-browser-timeout.regression.ps1` → exit 0, `MAPLE_SEASON_BROWSER_TIMEOUT_REGRESSION_PASS`, both cases.
- **`node --check` is the authority on the two `.mjs` files, not the IDE.** The editor's TypeScript service reported `Declaration or statement expected [1128]` at line numbers **past the end of the file** throughout this tranche. `node --check` returned clean on every edit, and both files execute.

### Open, not closed

- **Sol's findings 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9 on `7887fd3` are carried, with the next tranche already scoped.** 3 — the preflight regression still uses `-ErrorAction SilentlyContinue` on twelve port queries, which also makes SR-073's unqualified "every port query" claim false. 6 — the loopback prohibition in `verify-season-contract.mjs` is a case-sensitive substring, so it misses casing, whitespace, pipeline `Where-Object LocalAddress` narrowing, and alternate values. 7 — the "exactly one query" counter uses a case-sensitive `Get-NetTCPConnection\s+-`. 4 — "validate all before terminating any" still operates on one stale snapshot, since the listener set is enumerated once. 5 — the ten-second drain deadline does not bound the query itself. 8 — the tokenizer receipt can still be fabricated by direct assignment, and the expectations that judge it remain writable. 9 — two July assertions are substrings, not order or behaviour.
- **Finding 14's ledger half is answered here rather than edited there.** SR-073 called the repointed kill-by-PID drill new and omitted the genuinely new second-query drill, and overstated two textual guards. This ledger is append-only, so those corrections live in this entry: SR-073's six "new" static cases were five new plus one repointed, and the second-direct-query drill was new and unlisted.
- **The seven undrilled `mutation-drill:*` pins are still undrilled**, and all seven needles have now been validated as unique first-match hits at their intended lines, so the next tranche is mechanical rather than exploratory.
